Cross–pairs of operators

8
Actions on
T_{\lambda}
and
U_{\lambda}

The results of the preceding sections will now be applied to the distributions
T_{\lambda}
and
U_{\lambda}
.
Let us recall their definitions: for
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
it holds that

\langle\ T_{\lambda},\varphi(\underline{x})\ \rangle:=a_{m}\,\langle\ {\rm Fp}\,r^{\lambda+m-1}_{+},\Sigma^{0}[\varphi](r)\ \rangle_{r}

and

\langle\ U_{\lambda},\varphi(\underline{x})\ \rangle:=a_{m}\,\langle\ {\rm Fp}\,r^{\lambda+m-1}_{+},\Sigma^{1}[\varphi](r)\ \rangle_{r}

An alternative, and handy, notation could be
T_{\lambda}={\rm Fp}\,r^{\lambda}
and
U_{\lambda}=\underline{\omega}\,{\rm Fp}\,r^{\lambda}
.

Let us comment on these definitions; for more details we refer to the series of papers cited at the beginning of Section
1
.

A priori the distributions
T_{\lambda}
and
U_{\lambda}
are not defined at the simple poles of the one–dimensional distribution Fp
\;r^{\lambda+m-1}_{+}
on the
r
–axis, viz.,
\lambda=-m-n+1
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
. To cope with these singularities, the distributions Fp
\;r^{-n}_{+}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
are interpreted as the so–called
monomial pseudofunctions
(see
[
9
]
and
[
10
]
).

The distributions
T_{\lambda}
are standard scalar distributions well–known in harmonic analysis. They are radial and homogeneous of degree
\lambda
. As meromorphic functions of
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
they show genuine simple poles at
\lambda=-m,-m-2,-m-4,\ldots
. This is due to the fact that the singular points
\lambda=-m-2\ell-1
,
\ell=0,1,2,\ldots
are removable, since the spherical mean
\Sigma^{(0)}[\phi]
has its odd order derivatives vanishing at
r=0
. So we can define

\langle T_{-m-2\ell-1},\phi\rangle=\lim_{\mu\rightarrow-2\ell-2}a_{m}\langle{\rm Fp}\;r^{\mu}_{+},\Sigma^{(0)}[\phi]\rangle

but, remarkably, this limit is precisely
a_{m}\langle{\rm Fp}\;r^{-2\ell-2}_{+},\Sigma^{(0)}[\phi]\rangle
, with
Fp
\;r^{-2\ell-2}_{+}
the monomial pseudofunction.
The most important distribution is this family is
T_{-m+2}=\frac{1}{r^{m-2}}
, which is, up to a constant, the fundamental solution of the Laplace operator
\Delta
. Also note the special cases
T_{0}=1
,
T_{2\ell}=r^{2\ell}=(-1)^{\ell}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell}
, and
T_{2\ell+1}=r^{2\ell+1},\ell=0,1,2,\ldots
.

The distributions
U_{\lambda}
are typical Clifford analysis constructs. They are homogeneous of degree
\lambda
. As vector–valued meromorphic functions of
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
they show genuine simple poles at
\lambda=-m-1,-m-3,-m-5,\ldots
. This is due to the fact that the singular points
\lambda=-m-2\ell
,
\ell=0,1,2,\ldots
are removable, since the spherical mean
\Sigma^{(1)}[\phi]
has its even order derivatives vanishing at
r=0
. So we can define

\langle U_{-m-2\ell},\phi\rangle=\lim_{\mu\rightarrow-2\ell-1}a_{m}\langle{\rm Fp}\;r^{\mu}_{+},\Sigma^{(1)}[\phi]\rangle

but this limit is precisely
a_{m}\langle{\rm Fp}\;r^{-2\ell-1}_{+},\Sigma^{(1)}[\phi]\rangle
, with
Fp
\;r^{-2\ell-1}_{+}
the monomial pseudofunction.
The most important distribution in this family is

U_{-m+1}=\frac{\underline{\omega}}{r^{m-1}}=\frac{\underline{x}}{r^{m}}
which is, up to a constant, the fundamental solution of the Dirac operator
\underline{\partial}
. Also note the special cases
U_{0}=\underline{\omega}
,
U_{2\ell}=\underline{\omega}\,r^{2\ell}
and
U_{2\ell+1}=\underline{\omega}\,r^{2\ell+1}=(-1)^{\ell}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell+1},\ell=0,1,2,\ldots
.

It is important to note that although the distributions
T_{\lambda}
and
U_{\lambda}
are also defined in their respective singularities, these exceptional values do
not
turn these distributions into entire functions of the parameter
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
.

When restricted to the half–plane
\mathbb{R}e\,\lambda>-\,m
the distributions
T_{\lambda}
and
U_{\lambda}
are regular, i.e. they are locally integrable functions.
From
[
3
,
14
]
we know that a locally integrable function can be seen as a signumdistribution as well. This inspires the definition of the following two families of signumdistributions:

\langle\ ^{s}T_{\lambda},\underline{\omega}\,\varphi(\underline{x})\ \rangle:=\phantom{-}\,a_{m}\,\langle\ {\rm Fp}\,r^{\lambda+m-1}_{+},\Sigma^{1}[\varphi](r)\ \rangle_{r}

\langle\ ^{s}U_{\lambda},\underline{\omega}\,\varphi(\underline{x})\ \rangle:=-\,a_{m}\,\langle\ {\rm Fp}\,r^{\lambda+m-1}_{+},\Sigma^{0}[\varphi](r)\ \rangle_{r}

It is clear that

T_{\lambda}^{\,\vee}=\phantom{-}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}\quad,\quad^{s}U_{\lambda}^{\wedge}=\phantom{-}\,T_{\lambda}

and

U_{\lambda}^{\,\vee}=-\,^{s}T_{\lambda}\quad,\quad^{s}T_{\lambda}^{\wedge}\,=-\,U_{\lambda}

In this way
{}^{s}T_{\lambda}
inherits the simple poles of
U_{\lambda}
, viz.,
\lambda=-m-1,-m-3,-m-5,\ldots
, while
{}^{s}U_{\lambda}
inherits the simple poles of

T_{\lambda}
, viz.,
\lambda=-m,-m-2,-m-4,\ldots
.

As the distributions
T_{\lambda}
are radial, by Definition
10
the signumdistributions
{}^{s}U_{\lambda}
are signum–radial. As the signumdistributions
{}^{s}T_{\lambda}
are radial, by the same definition the distributions
U_{\lambda}
are signum–radial.

In the following subsections we will systematically compute the actions on
T_{\lambda}
,
U_{\lambda}
,
{}^{s}T_{\lambda}
and
{}^{s}U_{\lambda}
of all operators introduced in the precedings sections, paying attention to the uniqueness of the expressions obtained.

8.1
The operators
\underline{x}
,
r
and
r^{2}

The multiplication operator
\underline{x}
is a cartesian operator whose actions are uniquely determined, quite naturally. It holds for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
that

\boxed{\underline{x}\,T_{\lambda}=U_{\lambda+1}}\quad,\quad\boxed{\underline{x}\,U_{\lambda}=-\,T_{\lambda+1}}

(6)

Based on the commutative diagram

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccc}&&&{}_{-\underline{x}}&&&&&\\
&&T_{\lambda}&\longrightarrow&-\,U_{\lambda+1}&&&&\\[2.84526pt]
&&\hskip-22.76219pt{}_{-\underline{\omega}}&\hskip 5.69054pt{}\hskip 31.29802pt{}_{-r}&\hskip 17.07164pt{}_{-\underline{\omega}}&&&&\\[-5.69054pt]
&&\uparrow&\diagdown\hskip-3.69885pt\nearrow&\uparrow&&&&\\[-3.1298pt]
&&\downarrow&\diagup\hskip-3.69885pt\searrow&\downarrow&&&&\\[-5.69054pt]
&&\hskip-12.80373pt{}^{\underline{\omega}}&\hskip 5.69054pt{}\hskip 28.45274pt{}^{r}&\hskip 14.22636pt{}^{\underline{\omega}}&&&&\\[2.84526pt]
&&{}^{s}U_{\lambda}&\longrightarrow&{}^{s}T_{\lambda+1}&&&&\\[-2.84526pt]
&&&{}_{-\underline{x}}&&&&&\end{array}

we find the additional formulae

\boxed{r\,T_{\lambda}=\,^{s}T_{\lambda+1}}\quad{\rm and}\quad\boxed{r\,^{s}U_{\lambda}=U_{\lambda+1}}\quad,\quad\lambda\in\mathbb{C}

and

\boxed{\underline{x}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}=-\,^{s}T_{\lambda+1}}\quad,\quad\lambda\in\mathbb{C}

In a similar way, based on the commutative diagram

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccc}&&&{}_{\underline{x}}&&&&&\\
&&-\,U_{\lambda}&\longrightarrow&T_{\lambda+1}&&&&\\[2.84526pt]
&&\hskip-22.76219pt{}_{-\underline{\omega}}&\hskip 5.69054pt{}\hskip 31.29802pt{}_{r}&\hskip 17.07164pt{}_{-\underline{\omega}}&&&&\\[-5.69054pt]
&&\uparrow&\diagdown\hskip-3.69885pt\nearrow&\uparrow&&&&\\[-3.1298pt]
&&\downarrow&\diagup\hskip-3.69885pt\searrow&\downarrow&&&&\\[-5.69054pt]
&&\hskip-12.80373pt{}^{\underline{\omega}}&\hskip 5.69054pt{}\hskip 28.45274pt{}^{-r}&\hskip 14.22636pt{}^{\underline{\omega}}&&&&\\[2.84526pt]
&&{}^{s}T_{\lambda}&\longrightarrow&{}^{s}U_{\lambda+1}&&&&\\[-2.84526pt]
&&&{}_{\underline{x}}&&&&&\end{array}

we obtain the additional formulae

\boxed{r\,^{s}T_{\lambda}=T_{\lambda+1}}\quad{\rm and}\quad\boxed{r\,U_{\lambda}=\,^{s}U_{\lambda+1}}\quad,\quad\lambda\in\mathbb{C}

and also

\boxed{\underline{x}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}=\,^{s}U_{\lambda+1}}\quad,\quad\lambda\in\mathbb{C}

Iteration of the multiplication operator
\underline{x}
results into

\boxed{r^{2}\ T_{\lambda}=T_{\lambda+2}}\quad,\quad\boxed{r^{2}\,U_{\lambda}=U_{\lambda+2}}\quad,\quad\lambda\in\mathbb{C}

and

\boxed{r^{2}\ {}^{s}T_{\lambda}=\,^{s}T_{\lambda+2}}\quad,\quad\boxed{r^{2}\,{}^{s}U_{\lambda}=\,^{s}U_{\lambda+2}}\quad,\quad\lambda\in\mathbb{C}

The natural powers of
\underline{x}
are cartesian operators too. We find, for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
,

\displaystyle(-1)^{\ell}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell}\,T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=r^{2\ell}\,T_{\lambda}=T_{\lambda+2\ell}

\displaystyle(-1)^{\ell}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell+1}\,T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=(\underline{\omega}\,r^{2\ell+1})\,T_{\lambda}=U_{\lambda+2\ell+1}

and

\displaystyle(-1)^{\ell}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell}\,U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=r^{2\ell}\,U_{\lambda}=U_{\lambda+2\ell}

\displaystyle(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell+1}\,U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=-\,(\underline{\omega}\,r^{2\ell+1})\,U_{\lambda}=T_{\lambda+2\ell+1}

Through the appropriate commuative diagrams we find, for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
,

\displaystyle r^{2\ell}\,{}^{s}T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\,^{s}T_{\lambda+2\ell}

\displaystyle r^{2\ell}\,{}^{s}U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\,^{s}U_{\lambda+2\ell}

and

\displaystyle r^{2\ell+1}\,T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\,^{s}T_{\lambda+2\ell+1}

\displaystyle r^{2\ell+1}\,U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\,^{s}U_{\lambda+2\ell+1}

8.2
The operators
\underline{\partial}
and
\Delta

As does the multiplication operator
\underline{x}
, also the Dirac operator
\underline{\partial}
intertwines the
T_{\lambda}
and
U_{\lambda}
distribution families. It clearly is a cartesian operator whose action is uniquely determined. It holds that

\boxed{\underline{\partial}\,T_{\lambda}=\lambda\,U_{\lambda-1}}\quad,\quad\lambda\neq-m,-m-2,-m-4,\ldots

(7)

and

\boxed{\underline{\partial}\,U_{\lambda}=-(\lambda+m-1)\,T_{\lambda-1}}\quad,\quad\lambda\neq-m+1,-m-1,-m-3,\ldots

(8)

while for
\ell=0,1,2,\ldots

\boxed{\underline{\partial}\,T_{-m-2\ell}=-\,(m+2\ell)\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{1}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})}

(9)

and

\boxed{\underline{\partial}\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}=(2\ell)\,T_{-m-2\ell}+(-1)^{\ell-1}\,\frac{m+2\ell}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})}

(10)

with

C(m,\ell)=2^{2\ell+1}\,\ell!\,\frac{\Gamma(\frac{m}{2}+\ell+1)}{\Gamma(\frac{m}{2})}=2^{\ell}\,\ell!\,m(m+2)(m+4)\cdots(m+2\ell)

In particular, for
\ell=0
, it holds that

\boxed{\underline{\partial}\,T_{-m}=(-m)\,U_{-m-1}-\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})}

(11)

\boxed{\underline{\partial}\,U_{-m+1}=-\,a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})}

(12)

Formula (
12
) expresses the well–known fact that
-\,\frac{1}{m}\,U_{-m+1}
is indeed the fundamental solution of the Dirac operator
\underline{\partial}
.

Note also that

\underline{\partial}\,T_{0}=0\quad{\rm and}\quad\underline{\partial}\,\ln{r}=U_{-1}

Through the signum–pair of operators
(\underline{\partial},\underline{D})
the corresponding formulae for the signumdistributions
{}^{s}T_{\lambda}
and
{}^{s}U_{\lambda}
are readily obtained:

\boxed{\underline{D}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}=-\,\lambda\,^{s}T_{\lambda-1}}\quad,\quad\lambda\neq-m,-m-2,-m-4,\ldots

and

\boxed{\underline{D}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}=(\lambda+m-1)\,^{s}U_{\lambda-1}}\quad,\quad\lambda\neq-m+1,-m-1,-m-3,\ldots

while for
\ell=0,1,2,\ldots

\boxed{\underline{D}\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}=(m+2\ell)\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{1}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})}

and

\boxed{\underline{D}\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell+1}=-\,(2\ell)\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{m+2\ell}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})}

and in particular, for
\ell=0
,

\boxed{\underline{D}\,^{s}U_{-m}=m\,^{s}T_{-m-1}-\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})}

\boxed{\underline{D}\,^{s}T_{-m+1}=a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})}

Iteration of the Dirac operator
\underline{\partial}
results into formulae for the action of the Laplace operator on distributions. It holds that

\displaystyle\Delta\,T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\,(\lambda+m-2)\,T_{\lambda-2}\quad,\quad\lambda\neq-m+2,-m,-m-2,\ldots

(13)

\displaystyle\Delta\,U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\lambda-1)(\lambda+m-1)\,U_{\lambda-2}\quad,\quad\lambda\neq-m+1,-m-1,\ldots

(14)

and

\displaystyle\Delta\,T_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell)(2\ell+2)\,T_{-m-2\ell-2}+(-1)^{\ell}\,a_{m}\,\frac{(m+4\ell+2)(m+2\ell+2)}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(15)

\displaystyle\Delta\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell)(2\ell)\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell}\,a_{m}\,\frac{m+4\ell}{C(m,\ell)}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(16)

and, in particular, for
\ell=0

\displaystyle\Delta\,T_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2m\,T_{-m-2}+\frac{m+2}{2m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(17)

\displaystyle\Delta\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})

(18)

and also

\displaystyle\Delta\,T_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-2)\,a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(19)

this last formula expressing the fact that
-\,\frac{1}{m-2}\,\frac{1}{a_{m}}\,T_{-m+2}
is, indeed, the fundamental solution of the Laplace operator.

As an aside notice that continuing the iteration by the Dirac operator
\underline{\partial}
leads to the fundamental solutions of the natural powers of
\underline{\partial}
. We find

\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\,E_{2\ell}=\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\left(\frac{1}{2^{\ell-1}\,(\ell-1)!\,(m-2)(m-4)\cdots(m-2\ell)}\,\frac{1}{a_{m}}\,T_{-m+2\ell}\right)=\delta(\underline{x})

and

\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,E_{2\ell+1}=\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\left(-\,\frac{1}{2^{\ell}\,(\ell)!\,(m-2)(m-4)\cdots(m-2\ell)}\,\frac{1}{a_{m}}\,U_{-m+2\ell+1}\right)=\delta(\underline{x})

If the dimension
m
is odd then the above formulae are valid for all natural values of
\ell
. However if the dimension
m
is even, then these expressions are only valid for
\ell<m/2
; this already becomes clear from the fundamental solution
E_{m}
of the operator

\underline{\partial}^{m}
which is logarithmic in nature:

\underline{\partial}^{m}\,E_{m}=\underline{\partial}^{m}\left(-\,\frac{1}{2^{m-1}\,\pi^{m/2}\,\Gamma(m/2)}\,\ln r\right)=\delta(\underline{x})\quad,\quad m\ {\rm even}

More generally it holds for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
and still for
m
even, that, (see
[
7
]
),

\displaystyle E_{m+2k-1}

\displaystyle=\left(p_{2k-1}\,\ln r+q_{2k-1}\right)\,\frac{\pi^{\frac{m}{2}+k}}{\Gamma(\frac{m}{2}+k)}\,U_{2k-1}

\displaystyle E_{m+2k}

\displaystyle=\left(p_{2k}\,\ln r+q_{2k}\right)\,\frac{\pi^{\frac{m}{2}+k}}{\Gamma(\frac{m}{2}+k)}\,T_{2k}

the constants
(p_{2k-1},q_{2k-1})
and
(p_{2k},q_{2k})
satisfying the recurrence relations

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}p_{2k}&=&\displaystyle{\frac{1}{2k}}\ p_{2k-1}\\[5.69054pt]
q_{2k}&=&\displaystyle{\frac{1}{2k}\left(q_{2k-1}-\frac{1}{2k}\,p_{2k-1}\right)}\end{array}\right.

and

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}p_{2k+1}&=&\displaystyle{-\,\frac{1}{2\pi}}\ p_{2k}\\[5.69054pt]
q_{2k+1}&=&\displaystyle{-\,\frac{1}{2\pi}\left(q_{2k}-\frac{1}{m+2k}\,p_{2k}\right)}\end{array}\right.

with initial values

p_{0}=-\,\frac{1}{2^{m-1}\,\pi^{m}}\quad,\quad q_{0}=0

Through the signum–pair of operators
(\Delta,{\bf Z})
or, equivalently, by iteration of the action of the operator
\underline{D}
, we obtain the corresponding formulae for the operator
{\bf Z}
acting between signum–distributions:

\displaystyle{\bf Z}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\,(\lambda+m-2)\,^{s}U_{\lambda-2}\quad,\quad\lambda\neq-m+2,-m,-m-2,\ldots

(22)

\displaystyle{\bf Z}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\lambda-1)(\lambda+m-1)\,^{s}T_{\lambda-2}\quad,\quad\lambda\neq-m+1,-m-1,\ldots

(23)

and

\displaystyle{\bf Z}\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell)(2\ell+2)\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell-2}+(-1)^{\ell}\,a_{m}\,\frac{(m+4\ell+2)(m+2\ell+2)}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(24)

\displaystyle{\bf Z}\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell)(2\ell)\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,a_{m}\,\frac{m+4\ell}{C(m,\ell)}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(26)

and, in particular, for
\ell=0

\displaystyle{\bf Z}\,^{s}U_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2m\,^{s}U_{-m-2}+a_{m}\,\frac{m+2}{2m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(27)

\displaystyle{\bf Z}\,^{s}T_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{m}\,\partial_{r}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(28)

and also

\displaystyle{\bf Z}\,^{s}U_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-2)\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(29)

8.3
The operators
\mathbb{E}
,
\Gamma
and
\partial_{\underline{\omega}}

The operators
\mathbb{E}
and
\Gamma
are cartesian and their actions on the distributions
T_{\lambda}
and
U_{\lambda}
are uniquely determined.
Combining the actions of the operators
\underline{x}
and
\underline{\partial}
we find, through formula (
2
), the following formulae:

\boxed{\mathbb{E}\,T_{\lambda}=\lambda\,T_{\lambda}}\quad,\quad\boxed{\Gamma\,T_{\lambda}=0}\quad,\quad\lambda\neq-m,-m-2,\ldots

(30)

and

\boxed{\mathbb{E}\,U_{\lambda}=\lambda\,U_{\lambda}}\quad,\quad\boxed{\Gamma\,U_{\lambda}=(m-1)\,U_{\lambda}}\quad,\quad\lambda\neq-m+1,-m-1,\ldots

(31)

while for
\ell=0,1,2,\ldots

\boxed{\mathbb{E}\,T_{-m-2\ell}=-\,(m+2\ell)\,T_{-m-2\ell}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{m+2\ell}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})}\quad,\quad\boxed{\Gamma\,T_{-m-2\ell}=0}

(32)

and

\boxed{\mathbb{E}\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}=-\,(m+2\ell-1)\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{1}{C(m,\ell-1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell-1}\,\delta(\underline{x})}\quad,\quad\boxed{\Gamma\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}=(m-1)\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}}

(33)

and in particular, for
\ell=0
,

\boxed{\mathbb{E}\,T_{-m}=-\,m\,T_{-m}+a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})}\quad,\quad\boxed{\Gamma\,T_{-m}=0}

(34)

and

\boxed{\mathbb{E}\,U_{-m+1}=(-m+1)\,U_{-m+1}}\quad,\quad\boxed{\Gamma\,U_{-m+1}=(m-1)\,U_{-m+1}}

(35)

Notice that the result
\Gamma\,U_{\lambda}=(m-1)\,U_{\lambda}
holds for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
. This is no surprise since it was proved in
[
3
]
that for any signum–radial distribution
S
it holds that
\Gamma\,S=(m-1)\,S
.

Through the signum–pair of operators
(\mathbb{E},\mathbb{E})
the actions of the Euler operator on the associated signumdistributions are readily obtained:

\boxed{\mathbb{E}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}=\lambda\,^{s}U_{\lambda}}\quad,\quad\lambda\neq-m,-m-2,\ldots

and

\boxed{\mathbb{E}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}=\lambda\,^{s}T_{\lambda}}\quad,\quad\lambda\neq-m+1,-m-1,\ldots

while for
\ell=0,1,2,\ldots

\boxed{\mathbb{E}\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}=-\,(m+2\ell)\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{m+2\ell}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})}

and

\boxed{\mathbb{E}\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell+1}=-\,(m+2\ell-1)\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell+1}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{1}{C(m,\ell-1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell-1}\,\delta(\underline{x})}

and in particular, for
\ell=0
,

\boxed{\mathbb{E}\,^{s}U_{-m}=-\,m\,^{s}U_{-m}+a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})}

and

\boxed{\mathbb{E}\,^{s}T_{-m+1}=(-m+1)\,^{s}T_{-m+1}}

Through the signum–pair of operators
(\Gamma,-\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}}\,\underline{\omega})
the above formulae for the action of the operator
\Gamma
on the distributions
T_{\lambda}
immediately lead to the following results, valid for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
:

•

\partial_{\underline{\omega}}\,T_{\lambda}=0

•

\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}}\,\underline{\omega}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}=0

•

\partial_{\underline{\omega}}\,\underline{\omega}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}=0

the last formula leading to

\boxed{\Gamma\,^{s}U_{\lambda}=(m-1)\,^{s}U_{\lambda}}\quad,\quad\lambda\in\mathbb{C}

(36)

Similarly, the action of
\Gamma
on the distributions
U_{\lambda}
entails for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
:

•

\partial_{\underline{\omega}}\,U_{\lambda}=-\,(m-1)\,^{s}T_{\lambda}

•

\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}}\,\underline{\omega}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}=-\,(m-1)\,U_{\lambda}

•

\partial_{\underline{\omega}}\,\underline{\omega}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}=-\,(m-1)\,^{s}T_{\lambda}

this last formula leading to

\boxed{\Gamma\,^{s}T_{\lambda}=0}\quad,\quad\lambda\in\mathbb{C}

(37)

8.4
The operators
\partial_{\underline{\omega}}\,\underline{\omega}
,
\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}}\,\underline{\omega}
,
\Delta^{*}
and
{\bf Z}^{*}

As was stated in Proposition
1
the angular operators
\partial_{\underline{\omega}}^{2}
and
\Delta^{*}
are cartesian and their actions on (signum)distributions are uniquely determined.
As

\partial_{\underline{\omega}}\,\underline{\omega}=-\,(m-1)\,{\bf 1}+\Gamma

it holds for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
that

\partial_{\underline{\omega}}\,\underline{\omega}\,T_{\lambda}=-\,(m-1)\,T_{\lambda}

and through the commutative diagram

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccc}&&&{}_{\partial_{\underline{\omega}}\,\underline{\omega}}&&&&&\\
&&T_{\lambda}&\longrightarrow&-\,(m-1)\,T_{\lambda}&&&&\\[2.84526pt]
&&\hskip-22.76219pt{}_{-\underline{\omega}}&\hskip 5.69054pt{}\hskip 36.98857pt{}_{\partial_{\underline{\omega}}}&\hskip 17.07164pt{}_{-\underline{\omega}}&&&&\\[-5.69054pt]
&&\uparrow&\diagdown\hskip-3.69885pt\nearrow&\uparrow&&&&\\[-3.1298pt]
&&\downarrow&\diagup\hskip-3.69885pt\searrow&\downarrow&&&&\\[-5.69054pt]
&&\hskip-12.80373pt{}^{\underline{\omega}}&\hskip 5.69054pt{}\hskip 42.67912pt{}^{\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}}\,\underline{\omega}}&\hskip 14.22636pt{}^{\underline{\omega}}&&&&\\[2.84526pt]
&&{}^{s}U_{\lambda}&\longrightarrow&-\,(m-1)\,^{s}U_{\lambda}&&&&\\[-2.84526pt]
&&&{}_{\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}}}&&&&&\end{array}

we obtain for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}

•

\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}}\,\underline{\omega}\,T_{\lambda}=-\,(m-1)\,^{s}U_{\lambda}

•

\partial_{\underline{\omega}}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}=-\,(m-1)\,T_{\lambda}

meanwhile confirming formula (
36
), viz.

\Gamma\,^{s}U_{\lambda}=(m-1)\,^{s}U_{\lambda}

Similarly it holds for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
that

\partial_{\underline{\omega}}\,\underline{\omega}\,U_{\lambda}=0

and hence, through the commutative diagram

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccc}&&&{}_{\partial_{\underline{\omega}}\,\underline{\omega}}&&&&&\\
&&U_{\lambda}&\longrightarrow&0&&&&\\[2.84526pt]
&&\hskip-22.76219pt{}_{-\underline{\omega}}&\hskip 5.69054pt{}\hskip 36.98857pt{}_{\partial_{\underline{\omega}}}&\hskip 17.07164pt{}_{-\underline{\omega}}&&&&\\[-5.69054pt]
&&\uparrow&\diagdown\hskip-3.69885pt\nearrow&\uparrow&&&&\\[-3.1298pt]
&&\downarrow&\diagup\hskip-3.69885pt\searrow&\downarrow&&&&\\[-5.69054pt]
&&\hskip-12.80373pt{}^{\underline{\omega}}&\hskip 5.69054pt{}\hskip 42.67912pt{}^{\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}}\,\underline{\omega}}&\hskip 14.22636pt{}^{\underline{\omega}}&&&&\\[2.84526pt]
&&-\,^{s}T_{\lambda}&\longrightarrow&0&&&&\\[-2.84526pt]
&&&{}_{\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}}}&&&&&\end{array}

that, for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}

•

\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}}\,\underline{\omega}\,U_{\lambda}=0

•

\partial_{\underline{\omega}}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}=0

meanwhile confirming formula (
37
), viz.

\Gamma\,^{s}T_{\lambda}=0

For the actions of the Laplace–Beltrami operator
\Delta^{*}=\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}}-\partial_{\underline{\omega}}^{2}
and its signum–partner
{\bf Z}^{*}
, we now find, for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
,

\displaystyle\Delta^{*}\,T_{\lambda}=0\quad

\displaystyle{\rm and}

\displaystyle\quad{\bf Z}^{*}\,{}^{s}U_{\lambda}=0

(38)

\displaystyle\Delta^{*}\,U_{\lambda}=-\,(m-1)\,U_{\lambda}\quad

\displaystyle{\rm and}

\displaystyle\quad{\bf Z}^{*}\,{}^{s}T_{\lambda}=-\,(m-1)\,^{s}T_{\lambda}

(39)

\displaystyle\Delta^{*}\,{}^{s}T_{\lambda}=0\quad

\displaystyle{\rm and}

\displaystyle\quad{\bf Z}^{*}\,U_{\lambda}=0

(40)

\displaystyle\Delta^{*}\,{}^{s}U_{\lambda}=-\,(m-1)\,^{s}U_{\lambda}\quad

\displaystyle{\rm and}

\displaystyle\quad{\bf Z}^{*}\,T_{\lambda}=-\,(m-1)\,T_{\lambda}

(41)

8.5
The operators
\frac{1}{\underline{x}}
,
\frac{1}{r}
and
\frac{1}{r^{2}}

Division of a distribution by
\underline{x}
is a cartesian operation which leads, in general, to an equivalence class of distributions. However, in view of the results of Section
6
, we know that under the action of the operators
\frac{1}{\underline{x}}
and
\frac{1}{r}
only the distribution
U_{-m+1}
and the signumdistribution
{}^{s}T_{-m+1}
will have a non–unique result. The same holds for
U_{-m+1}
,
{}^{s}T_{-m+1}
,
T_{-m+2}
and
{}^{s}U_{-m+2}
under the action of the operator
\frac{1}{r^{2}}
.

We obtain the formulae

\boxed{\frac{1}{r}\,T_{\lambda}=\,^{s}T_{\lambda-1}}\quad{\rm and}\quad\boxed{\frac{1}{r}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}=U_{\lambda-1}}\quad,\quad\lambda\in\mathbb{C}

and

\boxed{\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,T_{\lambda}=-\,U_{\lambda-1}}\quad{\rm and}\quad\boxed{\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}=\,^{s}T_{\lambda-1}}\quad,\quad\lambda\in\mathbb{C}

and

\boxed{\frac{1}{r}\,U_{\lambda}=\,^{s}U_{\lambda-1}}\quad{\rm and}\quad\boxed{\frac{1}{r}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}=T_{\lambda-1}}\quad,\quad\lambda\neq-m+1

and also

\boxed{\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,U_{\lambda}=T_{\lambda-1}}\quad{\rm and}\quad\boxed{{\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}=-\,^{s}U_{\lambda-1}}}\quad,\quad\lambda\neq-m+1

For the exceptional case where
\lambda=-m+1
we obtain the following non–unique results:

\boxed{\frac{1}{r}\,U_{-m+1}=\,^{s}U_{-m}+\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})\,c}

\boxed{\frac{1}{r}\,^{s}T_{-m+1}=\,T_{-m}+\delta(\underline{x})\,c}

and

\boxed{\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,U_{-m+1}=\,T_{-m}+\delta(\underline{x})\,c}

\boxed{\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,^{s}T_{-m+1}=\,-\,^{s}U_{-m}-\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})\,c}

By iteration we find formulae for the divion by
r^{2}
:

\boxed{\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,T_{\lambda}=\,\phantom{{}^{s}}T_{\lambda-2}}\qquad\boxed{\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}=\,^{s}U_{\lambda-2}}\quad,\quad\lambda\neq-m+2

and

\boxed{\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}=\,^{s}T_{\lambda-2}}\qquad\boxed{\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,U_{\lambda}=\,\phantom{{}^{s}}U_{\lambda-2}}\quad,\quad\lambda\neq-m+1

For the exceptional case
\lambda=-m+2
we have

\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,T_{-m+2}=-\,\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,T_{-m+2}=\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,U_{-m+1}

whence

\boxed{\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,T_{-m+2}=\,T_{-m}+\delta(\underline{x})\,c}\qquad\boxed{\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,^{s}U_{-m+2}=\,^{s}U_{-m}+\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})\,c}

For the exceptional case
\lambda=-m+1
we have

\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,U_{-m+1}=-\,\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,U_{-m+1}=-\,\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,(T_{-m}+\delta(\underline{x})\,c)

whence

\boxed{\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,^{s}T_{-m+1}=\,^{s}T_{-m-1}-\frac{1}{m}\,\partial_{r}\,\delta(\underline{x})\,c}\qquad\boxed{\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,U_{-m+1}=U_{-m-1}-\frac{1}{m}\,\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})\,c}

The action of the components of the operator
\frac{1}{\underline{x}}
, viz.
(-\,\frac{x_{j}}{r^{2}}),j=1,\ldots,m
, on all
T_{\lambda}
and
U_{\lambda}
is uniquely determined except for
U_{-m+1}
. We have readily

\boxed{(-\,\frac{x_{j}}{r^{2}})\,T_{\lambda}=-\,x_{j}\,T_{\lambda-2}}\qquad\boxed{(-\,\frac{x_{j}}{r^{2}})\,^{s}U_{\lambda}=-\,x_{j}\,^{s}U_{\lambda-2}}\quad,\quad\lambda\in\mathbb{C}

Further for
\lambda\neq-m+1
we have

\boxed{(-\,\frac{x_{j}}{r^{2}})\,U_{\lambda}=-\,x_{j}\,U_{\lambda-2}}\qquad\boxed{(-\,\frac{x_{j}}{r^{2}})\,^{s}T_{\lambda}=-\,x_{j}\,^{s}T_{\lambda-2}}\quad,\quad\lambda\neq-m+1

while
for the exceptional case
\lambda=-m+1
, it holds that

(-\,\frac{x_{j}}{r^{2}})\,U_{-m+1}=-\,x_{j}\,(U_{-m-1}-\frac{1}{m}\,\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})\,c)

whence

\boxed{(-\,\frac{x_{j}}{r^{2}})\,U_{-m+1}=-\,x_{j}\,U_{-m-1}+\frac{1}{m}\,x_{j}\,\underline{\partial}\,\delta(\underline{x})\,c}\qquad\boxed{(-\,\frac{x_{j}}{r^{2}})\,^{s}T_{-m+1}=-\,x_{j}\,^{s}T_{-m-1}-\omega_{j}\,\delta(\underline{x})\,c}

or

\boxed{(-\,\frac{x_{j}}{r^{2}})\,U_{-m+1}=-\,x_{j}\,U_{-m-1}-\frac{1}{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})\,c\,e_{j}}\qquad\boxed{(-\,\frac{x_{j}}{r^{2}})\,^{s}T_{-m+1}=-\,x_{j}\,^{s}T_{-m-1}+\frac{1}{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})\,c\,e_{j}}

8.6
The operators
\omega_{j},j=1,\ldots,m

The multiplication operator
\omega_{j},j=1,\ldots,m
is a spherical operator whose actions are uniquely determined, see Definition
4
in Section
5
. It holds for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
that

\omega_{j}\,T_{\lambda}=\{\underline{\omega}\,T_{\lambda}\}_{j}=\{^{s}U_{\lambda}\}_{j}=x_{j}\,^{s}T_{\lambda-1}

and, through signum–association,

\omega_{j}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}=x_{j}\,U_{\lambda-1}

It is verified at once that

\underline{\omega}\,T_{\lambda}=\Sigma_{j=1}^{m}\,e_{j}\,\omega_{j}\,T_{\lambda}=\Sigma_{j=1}^{m}\,e_{j}\,x_{j}\,^{s}T_{\lambda-1}=\underline{x}\,^{s}T_{\lambda-1}=\,^{s}U_{\lambda}

and,

\underline{\omega}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}=\Sigma_{j=1}^{m}\,e_{j}\,\omega_{j}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}=\Sigma_{j=1}^{m}\,e_{j}\,x_{j}\,U_{\lambda-1}=\underline{x}\,U_{\lambda-1}=-\,T_{\lambda}

In a similar way we find that

\omega_{j}\,U_{\lambda}=\sum_{k}\,\omega_{j}\,\{U_{\lambda}\}_{k}\,e_{k}=\sum_{k}\,\omega_{j}\,x_{k}\,T_{\lambda-1}\,e_{k}=\sum_{k}\,x_{k}\,x_{j}\,^{s}T_{\lambda-2}\,e_{k}=x_{j}\,^{s}U_{\lambda-1}

and, through signum–association,

\omega_{j}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}=x_{j}\,T_{\lambda-1}

By iteration it follows, for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
, that

\displaystyle\omega_{k}\,\omega_{j}\,T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=x_{j}\,x_{k}\,T_{\lambda-2}

\displaystyle\omega_{k}\,\omega_{j}\,U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=x_{j}\,x_{k}\,U_{\lambda-2}

\displaystyle\omega_{k}\,\omega_{j}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=x_{j}\,x_{k}\,^{s}T_{\lambda-2}

\displaystyle\omega_{k}\,\omega_{j}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=x_{j}\,x_{k}\,^{s}U_{\lambda-2}

8.7
The operators
\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r}
,
\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}}
and
\partial_{r}

As we know how to divide by
\underline{x}
the distributions
T_{\lambda}
and
U_{\lambda}
, we are ready now to compute the actions of the radial and angular parts of the Dirac operator through the procedures (
3
) and (
4
) respectively. In general the results of those actions are equivalence classes of distributions. However we know from Section
6
that we may expect uniquely determined results, except for the distribution
U_{-m+1}
and the signumdistribution
{}^{s}T_{-m+1}
.

First we tackle the distributions
T_{\lambda}
.
For
\lambda\neq-m,-m-2,\ldots
we find that
(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,T_{\lambda}
is uniquely determined by

(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,T_{\lambda}=-\,\left[\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,\mathbb{E}\,T_{\lambda}\right]=-\,\left[\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,\lambda\,T_{\lambda}\right]==-\,\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,\lambda\,T_{\lambda}=\lambda\,U_{\lambda-1}

In view of (
7
) it follows that

(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,T_{\lambda}=0\quad,\quad\lambda\neq-m,-m-2,\ldots

For the exceptional cases we find that the distributions
(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,T_{-m-2\ell},\ell=0,1,2,\ldots
, are uniquely determined by

\displaystyle(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,T_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\left[\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,\mathbb{E}\,T_{-m-2\ell}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\left[\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,\left(-\,(m+2\ell)\,T_{-m-2\ell}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{m+2\ell}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m+2\ell)\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{1}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

In view of (
9
) it follows that

(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,T_{-m-2\ell}=0,\quad,\quad\ell=0,1,2,\ldots

In particular,
for
\lambda=-m
, we have

\displaystyle(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,T_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\left[\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,\mathbb{E}\,T_{-m}\right]=-\,\left[\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,(-m\,T_{-m}+a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x}))\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-m)\,U_{-m-1}-\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})

and

(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,T_{-m}=0

which are in accordance with (
11
).

Notice that the formula

\boxed{(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,T_{\lambda}=0}

thus holds for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
.

Through the signum–pair of operators
[\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r},\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r}]
and the corresponding commutative diagrams we then find the following formulae.

For
\lambda\neq-m,-m-2,\ldots
it holds that

•

\partial_{r}\,T_{\lambda}=\lambda\,^{s}T_{\lambda-1}

•

\partial_{r}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}=\lambda\,U_{\lambda-1}

•

(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,^{s}U_{\lambda}=-\,\lambda\,^{s}T_{\lambda-1}

For the exceptional values
\lambda=-\,m-2\ell,\ell=0,1,2,\ldots
it holds that

•

\partial_{r}\,T_{-m-2\ell}=-\,(m+2\ell)\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{1}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

•

\partial_{r}\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}=-\,(m+2\ell)\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{1}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

•

(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}=(m+2\ell)\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{1}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

and in particular, for
\ell=0
,

•

\partial_{r}\,T_{-m}=(-m)\,^{s}T_{-m-1}-\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\partial_{r}\,\delta(\underline{x})

•

\partial_{r}\,^{s}U_{-m}=(-m)\,U_{-m-1}-\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}\,\delta(\underline{x})

•

(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,^{s}U_{-m}=m\,^{s}T_{-m-1}+\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\partial_{r}\,\delta(\underline{x})

Through the signum–pair of operators
[\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}},-\,\frac{1}{r}\,\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}}\,\underline{\omega}]
and the corresponding commutative diagrams we obtain that for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}

•

\frac{1}{r}\,\Gamma\,T_{\lambda}=0

•

\frac{1}{r}\,\Gamma\,^{s}U_{\lambda}=(m-1)\,U_{\lambda-1}

•

\boxed{(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,^{s}U_{\lambda}=-\,(m-1)\,^{s}T_{\lambda-1}}

Now let us turn our attention to the distributions
U_{\lambda}
.

For
\lambda\neq-m+1,-m-1,\ldots
we find the unique expressions

(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,U_{\lambda}=-\,\left[\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,\mathbb{E}\,U_{\lambda}\right]=-\,\left[\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,\lambda\,U_{\lambda}\right]=-\,\lambda\,T_{\lambda-1}

and

(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,U_{\lambda}=-\,\left[\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,\Gamma\,U_{\lambda}\right]=-\,\left[\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,(m-1)\,U_{\lambda}\right]=-\,(m-1)\,T_{\lambda-1}

which are in accordance with (
8
).

In the exceptional cases we find, using (
33
), for
\ell=1,2,\ldots
, the unique expressions

\displaystyle(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\left[\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,\mathbb{E}\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\left[\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,\left(-\,(m+2\ell-1)\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{1}{C(m,\ell-1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell-1}\,\delta(\underline{x})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell-1)\,T_{-m-2\ell}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{m+2\ell}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})

and

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\left[\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,\Gamma\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\left[\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,(m-1)\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,T_{-m-2\ell}

which are in accordance with (
10
).

In the particular case where
\lambda=-m+1
we find, using (
35
), the entangled expressions

\displaystyle(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\left[\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,\mathbb{E}\,U_{-m+1}\right]=-\,\left[\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,(-m+1)\,U_{-m+1}\right]

(42)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m-1)\,T_{-m}+\delta(\underline{x})\,c_{1}

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\left[\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,\Gamma\,U_{-m+1}\right]=-\,\left[\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,(m-1)\,U_{-m+1}\right]

(43)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,T_{-m}+\delta(\underline{x})\,c_{2}

where, seen (
12
), the arbitrary constants
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
must satisfy the entanglement condition

c_{1}+c_{2}=-\,a_{m}

(44)

Remark 7
.

Just keep in mind that in subsection 8.8 we will fix a particular choice for the constants
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
satisfying (
44
) in this way
defining
both
(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,U_{-m+1}
and
(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,U_{-m+1}
.

Through the actions of the operators
\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r}
and
\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}}
it is possible to recover the formuale for the action of the Laplace operator.

•

For
\lambda\neq-m+2,-m,-m-2,\ldots
one has

\displaystyle(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})^{2}\,T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,(\lambda\,U_{\lambda-1})=-\,\lambda\,(\lambda-1)\,T_{\lambda-2}

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,(\lambda\,U_{\lambda-1})=-\,\lambda\,(m-1)\,T_{\lambda-2}

Keeping in mind that
(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,T_{\lambda}=0
, it is confirmed that

\Delta\,T_{\lambda}=\lambda\,(\lambda+m-2)\,T_{\lambda-2}

•

For
\lambda=-m+2
one has

\displaystyle(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})^{2}\,T_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,((-m+2)\,U_{-m+1})=-\,(m-2)\,((m-1)\,T_{-m}-a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x}))

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,T_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,((-m+2)\,U_{-m+1})=(m-2)(m-1)\,T_{-m}

confirming that

\Delta\,T_{-m+2}=-\,(m-2)\,a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})

•

For
\lambda=-m-2\ell
one has

\displaystyle(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})^{2}\,T_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,\left(-\,(m+2\ell)\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{(2\ell+2)(m+2\ell+2)}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})\right)

\displaystyle=-\,(m+2\ell)(m+2\ell+1)\,T_{-m-2\ell-2}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{m+2\ell+2}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,(2m+4\ell+1)\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,T_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,\left(-\,(m+2\ell)\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{(2\ell+2)(m+2\ell+2)}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})\right)

\displaystyle=-\,(m+2\ell)(1-m)\,T_{-m-2\ell-2}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{m+2\ell+2}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,(1-m)\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

confirming that

\Delta\,T_{-m-2\ell}=(m+2\ell)(2\ell+2)\,T_{-m-2\ell-2}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{(m+4\ell+2)(m+2\ell+2)}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+2}\,\delta(\underline{x})\\

•

For
\lambda\neq-m+1,-m-1,-m-3,\ldots
one has

\displaystyle(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})^{2}\,U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,(-\,\lambda\,T_{\lambda-1})=-\,\lambda\,(\lambda-1)\,U_{\lambda-2}

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,(-\,\lambda\,T_{\lambda-1})=0

\displaystyle(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,(-\,(m-1)\,T_{\lambda-1})=-\,(m-1)\,(\lambda-1)\,U_{\lambda-2}

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,(-\,(m-1)\,T_{\lambda-1})=0

confirming that

\Delta\,U_{\lambda}=(\lambda-1)(\lambda+m-1)\,U_{\lambda-2}

•

For
\lambda=-m+1
one has

\displaystyle(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})^{2}\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,((m-1)\,T_{-m}-a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x}))=-\,(m-1)m\,U_{-m-1}-\frac{2m-1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,((m-1)\,T_{-m}-a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x}))=0

\displaystyle(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,(-\,(m-1)\,T_{-m})=(m-1)m\,U_{-m-1}+\frac{m-1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,(-\,(m-1)\,T_{-m})=0

confirming that

\Delta\,U_{-m+1}=a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}\,\delta(\underline{x})

For
\lambda=-m-2\ell+1
one has

\displaystyle(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})^{2}\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=-\,(m+2\ell-1)(m+2\ell)\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{2m+4\ell-1}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=(m+2\ell)(m-1)\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}-(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{m-1}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=0

confirming that

\Delta\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}=(m+2\ell)(2\ell)\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell}\,a_{m}\,\frac{m+4\ell}{C(m,\ell)}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

Through the signum–pair of operators
[\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r},\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r}]
and the corresponding commutative diagrams, we find the following formulae.

For
\lambda\neq-m+1,-m-1,\ldots
it holds that

•

\partial_{r}\,U_{\lambda}=\lambda\,^{s}U_{\lambda-1}

•

\partial_{r}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}=\lambda\,T_{\lambda-1}

•

(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,^{s}T_{\lambda}=\lambda\,^{s}U_{\lambda-1}

For the exceptional values
\lambda=-m-2\ell+1,\ell=1,2,\ldots
it holds that

•

\partial_{r}\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}=-\,(m+2\ell-1)\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{m+2\ell}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})

•

\partial_{r}\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell+1}=-\,(m+2\ell-1)\,T_{-m-2\ell}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{m+2\ell}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})

•

(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell+1}=-\,(m+2\ell-1)\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{m+2\ell}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})

while for
\ell=0
it holds that

•

\partial_{r}\,U_{-m+1}=-\,(m-1)\,^{s}U_{-m}-\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})\,c_{1}

•

\partial_{r}\,^{s}T_{-m+1}=-\,(m-1)\,T_{-m}-\delta(\underline{x})\,c_{1}

•

(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,^{s}T_{-m+1}=-\,(m-1)\,^{s}U_{-m}-\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})\,c_{1}

Through the signum–pair of operators
[\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}},-\,\frac{1}{r}\,\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}}\,\underline{\omega}]
and the corresponding commutative diagrams, we find the following formulae.

For
\lambda\neq-m+1,-m-1,\ldots
it holds that

•

(-\,\frac{1}{r}\,\Gamma+(m-1)\,\frac{1}{r})\,^{s}T_{\lambda}=(m-1)\,T_{\lambda-1}

•

\frac{1}{r}\,\Gamma\,U_{\lambda}=(m-1)\,^{s}U_{\lambda-1}

•

(-\,\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}}+(m-1)\,\frac{1}{r}\,\underline{\omega})\,^{s}T_{\lambda}=(m-1)\,^{s}U_{\lambda-1}

or equivalently

•

\frac{1}{r}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}=T_{\lambda-1}

•

\frac{1}{r}\,\Gamma\,U_{\lambda}=(m-1)\,^{s}U_{\lambda-1}

•

\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}=0

For the exceptional values
\lambda=-m-2\ell+1,\ell=1,2,\ldots
it holds that

•

(-\,\frac{1}{r}\,\Gamma+(m-1)\,\frac{1}{r})\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell+1}=(m-1)\,T_{m-2\ell}

•

\frac{1}{r}\,\Gamma\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}=(m-1)\,^{s}U_{m-2\ell}

•

(-\,\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}}+(m-1)\,\frac{1}{r}\,\underline{\omega})\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell+1}=(m-1)\,^{s}U_{m-2\ell}

or equivalently

•

\frac{1}{r}\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell+1}=T_{m-2\ell}

•

\frac{1}{r}\,\Gamma\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}=(m-1)\,^{s}U_{m-2\ell}

•

\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}}\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell+1}=0

For the exceptional value
\lambda=-m+1
it holds that

•

(-\,\frac{1}{r}\,\Gamma+(m-1)\,\frac{1}{r})\,^{s}T_{-m+1}=(m-1)\,T_{-m}-\delta(\underline{x})\,c_{2}

•

\frac{1}{r}\,\Gamma\,U_{-m+1}=(m-1)\,^{s}U_{-m}+\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})\,c_{2}

•

(-\,\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}}+(m-1)\,\frac{1}{r}\,\underline{\omega})\,^{s}T_{-m+1}=(m-1)\,^{s}U_{-m}-\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})\,c_{2}

or, equivalently,

•

(m-1)\,\frac{1}{r}\,^{s}T_{-m+1}=(m-1)\,T_{-m}-\delta(\underline{x})\,c_{2}

•

\frac{1}{r}\,\Gamma\,U_{-m+1}=(m-1)\,^{s}U_{-m}+\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})\,c_{2}

•

(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,^{s}T_{-m+1}=0

Notice that the formula

\boxed{(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,^{s}T_{\lambda}=0}

is valid for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
.

As expected
U_{-m+1}
is the only distribution in the whole of the two families of distributions
T_{\lambda}
and
U_{\lambda}
,
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
, for which the actions of the operators
\underline{\partial}_{rad}
and
\underline{\partial}_{ang}
are
not
uniquely determined, and a similar observation holds for the signumdistribution
{}^{s}T_{-m+1}
. Nothing prevents us from defining these actions by choosing ourselves specific values for the arbitrary constants involved. However, in order to avoid possible inconsistencies, we will, for the moment, not do so and keep the constants
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
arbitrary but constraint by the entanglement condition (
44
), viz.:
c_{1}+c_{2}=-\,a_{m}
.
Notice that this is made possible by the fact that
\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,U_{-m+1}
was expressed as an equivalence class of distributions, viz.

\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,U_{-m+1}=T_{-m}+\delta(\underline{x})\,c

which is really necessary since fixing here and now a particular value for this arbitrary constant
c
would lead to the impossible result
\underline{\partial}\,U_{-m+1}=0
.

8.8
The operators
\underline{D}
and
{\bf Z}

Combining the results obtained in the preceding Subsection
8.7
for the actions of the radial and angular parts of the Dirac operator, we obtain the action of the operator
\underline{D}
on the distributions
T_{\lambda}
and
U_{\lambda}
and of the Dirac operator
\underline{\partial}
on the signumdistributions
{}^{s}T_{\lambda}
and
{}^{s}U_{\lambda}
. These results should match each other through the signum–pair of operators
[\underline{D},\underline{\partial}]
. We expect
U_{-m+1}
to be the only distribution for which the
\underline{D}
–action is not uniquely determined and the same for the signumdistribution
{}^{s}T_{-m+1}
with respect to the
\underline{\partial}
–action. Follows an overview of the results, recalling, for completeness’ sake, some formulae already obtained in the preceding subsections.

First we consider the case of the distributions
T_{\lambda}
and the associated signumdistributions
{}^{s}U_{\lambda}
.

For
\boxed{\lambda\neq-m,-m-2,\ldots}
it holds that

\displaystyle(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\,U_{\lambda-1}

(45)

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

(46)

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\,U_{\lambda-1}

(47)

\displaystyle\underline{D}\,T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\lambda+m-1)\,U_{\lambda-1}

(48)

and

\displaystyle(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,^{s}U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\lambda\,^{s}T_{\lambda-1}

(49)

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,^{s}U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,^{s}T_{\lambda-1}

(50)

\displaystyle\underline{D}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\lambda\,^{s}T_{\lambda-1}

(51)

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(\lambda+m-1)\,^{s}T_{\lambda-1}

(52)

For
\boxed{\lambda=-m}
it holds that

\displaystyle(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,T_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-m\,U_{-m-1}-\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(53)

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,T_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

(54)

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,T_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-m\,U_{-m-1}-\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(55)

\displaystyle\underline{D}\,T_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,U_{-m-1}-\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(56)

and

\displaystyle(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,^{s}U_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m\,^{s}T_{-m-1}+\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\partial_{r}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(57)

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,^{s}U_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,^{s}T_{-m-1}

(58)

\displaystyle\underline{D}\,^{s}U_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m\,^{s}T_{-m-1}+\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\partial_{r}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(59)

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,^{s}U_{-m}

\displaystyle=

{}^{s}T_{-m-1}+\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\partial_{r}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(60)

More generally for
\boxed{\lambda=-m-2\ell}\,,\ell=0,1,2,\ldots
, it holds that

\displaystyle(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,T_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m+2\ell)\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{1}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(61)

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,T_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

(62)

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,T_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m+2\ell)\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{1}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(63)

\displaystyle\underline{D}\,T_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(2\ell+1)\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{1}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(64)

and

\displaystyle(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell)\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{1}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(65)

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-1}

(66)

\displaystyle\underline{D}\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell)\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{1}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(67)

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2\ell+1)\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{1}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(68)

Now we treat the case of the distributions
U_{\lambda}
and the associated signumdistributions
{}^{s}T_{\lambda}
.

For
\boxed{\lambda\neq-m+1,-m-1,\ldots}
it holds that

\displaystyle(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\lambda\,T_{\lambda-1}

(69)

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,T_{\lambda-1}

(70)

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(\lambda+m-1)\,T_{\lambda-1}

(71)

\displaystyle\underline{D}\,U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\lambda\,T_{\lambda-1}

(72)

and

\displaystyle(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,^{s}T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\,^{s}U_{\lambda-1}

(73)

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,^{s}T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

(74)

\displaystyle\underline{D}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\lambda+m-1)\,^{s}U_{\lambda-1}

(75)

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\,^{s}U_{\lambda-1}

(76)

For
\boxed{\lambda=-m-2\ell+1}\,,\ell=1,2,\ldots
, it holds that

\displaystyle(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell-1)\,T_{-m-2\ell}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{m+2\ell}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(77)

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,T_{-m-2\ell}

(78)

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2\ell)\,T_{-m-2\ell}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{m+2\ell}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(79)

\displaystyle\underline{D}\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell-1)\,T_{-m-2\ell}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{m+2\ell}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(80)

and

\displaystyle(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m+2\ell-1)\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{m+2\ell}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(81)

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

(82)

\displaystyle\underline{D}\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(2\ell)\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{m+2\ell}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(83)

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m+2\ell-1)\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{m+2\ell}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(84)

For the exceptional case
\boxed{\lambda=-m+1}
we find that

\displaystyle(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m-1)\,T_{-m}+\delta(\underline{x})\,c_{1}

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,T_{-m}+\delta(\underline{x})\,c_{2}

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle\underline{D}\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m-1)\,T_{-m}+\delta(\underline{x})\,c^{*}

and

\displaystyle(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,^{s}T_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,^{s}U_{-m}-\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})\,c_{1}

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,^{s}T_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\underline{D}\,^{s}T_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})\,a_{m}

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,^{s}T_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,^{s}U_{-m}-\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})\,c^{*}

Apparently the constant
c^{*}
may be chosen freely, whereas the constants
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
must satisfy the entanglement condition (
44
), viz.

c_{1}+c_{2}=-\,a_{m}

We will now fix these arbitrary constants ourselves, which means that we are going to
define

(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,U_{-m+1}
,
(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,U_{-m+1}
and
\underline{D}\,U_{-m+1}
. Quite naturally we have to be very cautious and constantly watch over the concistency. We make the following choice:

c^{*}=c_{1}=-\,a_{m}\quad,\quad c_{2}=0

Are there arguments for this particular choice? Concerning the choice for
c^{*}
, one can see that for general
\lambda
the expressions for
(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,U_{\lambda}
and
\underline{D}\,U_{\lambda}
coincide, inspiring the choice
c^{*}=c_{1}
. Next, putting
\ell=0
in the expression for
(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}
results into

(m-1)\,T_{-m}-a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})

which then can serve as the definition for
(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,U_{-m+1}
making
c_{1}=-\,a_{m}
and
c_{2}=0
. Hence the following definition.

Definition 12
.

One defines

\displaystyle(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m-1)\,T_{-m}-a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(85)

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,T_{-m}

(86)

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(87)

\displaystyle\underline{D}\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m-1)\,T_{-m}-a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(88)

and

\displaystyle(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,^{s}T_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,^{s}U_{-m}+a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(89)

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,^{s}T_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

(90)

\displaystyle\underline{D}\,^{s}T_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(91)

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,^{s}T_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,^{s}U_{-m}+a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(92)

Notice by the way that formula (
86
) matches the corresponding formula for all other values of the parameter
\lambda
, viz.

(\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}})\,U_{\lambda}=-\,(m-1)\,T_{\lambda-1}

Iteration of the operators
\underline{\partial}
and
\underline{D}
yield the following formulae involving the Laplace and
{\bf Z}
operators. These results should match each other pairwise through the signum–pairs of operators
(\Delta,{\bf Z})
and
[{\bf Z},\Delta]
. Based on the results of Section
6
we expect the distributions
T_{-m+2}
and
U_{-m+1}
to have non–unique results under the action of the operator
{\bf Z}
; the same holds for the signumdistributions
{}^{s}U_{-m+2}
and
{}^{s}T_{-m+1}
under the action of the Laplace operator
\Delta
.

First we consider the case of the distributions
T_{\lambda}
and the associated signumdistributions
{}^{s}U_{\lambda}
.

For
\boxed{\lambda\neq-m+2,-m,-m-2,\ldots}
we already found formulae (
13
) and (
22
), viz.

\displaystyle\Delta\,T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\,(\lambda+m-2)\,T_{\lambda-2}

\displaystyle{\bf Z}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\,(\lambda+m-2)\,^{s}U_{\lambda-2}

and now it also holds that

\displaystyle{\bf Z}\,T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\lambda-1)(\lambda+m-1)\,T_{\lambda-2}

(93)

\displaystyle\Delta\,^{s}U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\lambda-1)(\lambda+m-1)\,^{s}U_{\lambda-2}

(94)

For
\boxed{\lambda=-m+2}
we already found formulae (
19
) and (
29
), viz.

\displaystyle\Delta\,T_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(m-2)\,a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle{\bf Z}\,^{s}U_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(m-2)\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})

and now it also holds that

\displaystyle{\bf Z}\,T_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(m-1)\,T_{-m}\,+a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(95)

\displaystyle\Delta\,^{s}U_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(m-1)\,^{s}U_{-m}+a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(96)

For
\boxed{\lambda=-m}
we already found formulae (
17
) and (
27
), viz.

\displaystyle\Delta\,T_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2m\,T_{-m-2}+(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{m})\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle{\bf Z}\,^{s}U_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2m\,^{s}U_{-m-2}+(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{m})\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

and now it also holds that

\displaystyle{\bf Z}\,T_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+1)\,T_{-m-2}+\frac{m+2}{2m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(97)

\displaystyle\Delta\,^{s}U_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+1)\,^{s}U_{-m-2}+\frac{m+2}{2m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(98)

More generally for
\boxed{\lambda=-m-2\ell}\,,\ell=0,1,2,\ldots
we already found formulae (
15
) and (
24
), viz.

\displaystyle\Delta\,T_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell)(2\ell+2)\,T_{-m-2\ell-2}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{(m+4\ell+2)(m+2\ell+2)}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle{\bf Z}\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell)(2\ell+2)\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell-2}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{(m+4\ell+2)(m+2\ell+2)}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

and now it also holds that

\displaystyle{\bf Z}\,T_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell+1)(2\ell+1)\,T_{-m-2\ell-2}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{(m+4\ell+2)(m+2\ell+2)}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(99)

\displaystyle\Delta\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell+1)(2\ell+1)\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell-2}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{(m+4\ell+2)(m+2\ell+2)}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(100)

Now we treat the case of the distributions
U_{\lambda}
and the associated signumdistributions
{}^{s}T_{\lambda}
.

For
\boxed{\lambda\neq-m+1,-m-1,\ldots}
we already found formulae (
14
) and (
23
), viz.

\displaystyle\Delta\,U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\lambda-1)(\lambda+m-1)\,U_{\lambda-2}

\displaystyle{\bf Z}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\lambda-1)(\lambda+m-1)\,^{s}T_{\lambda-2}

and now it also holds that

\displaystyle{\bf Z}\,U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\,(\lambda+m-2)\,U_{\lambda-2}

(101)

\displaystyle\Delta\,^{s}T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\,(\lambda+m-2)\,^{s}T_{\lambda-2}

(102)

For
\boxed{\lambda=-m+1}
we already found formulae (
18
) and (
28
), viz.

\displaystyle\Delta\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle{\bf Z}\,^{s}T_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{m}\,\partial_{r}\,\delta(\underline{x})

and now it also holds that

\displaystyle{\bf Z}\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m-1)\,U_{-m-1}+a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(103)

\displaystyle\Delta\,^{s}T_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m-1)\,^{s}T_{-m-1}-a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(104)

More generally for
\boxed{\lambda=-m-2\ell+1}\,,\ell=1,2,\ldots
we already found formulae (
16
) and (
26
), viz.

\displaystyle\Delta\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell)(2\ell)\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{m+4\ell}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle{\bf Z}\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell)(2\ell)\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{m+4\ell}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

and now it also holds that

\displaystyle{\bf Z}\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell-1)(2\ell+1)\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{m+4\ell}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(105)

\displaystyle\Delta\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell-1)(2\ell+1)\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{m+4\ell}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

(106)

Note that when putting
\ell=0
in (

) then (
103
) is obtained.

8.9
The operators
\partial_{r}^{2}
,
\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{r}
,
\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,\Delta^{*}
and
\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,{\bf Z}^{*}

In general the separate actions of the three components of the Laplace operator expressed in spherical co-ordinates

\Delta=\partial_{r}^{2}+(m-1)\,\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{r}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,\Delta^{*}

and of the associated signum–operator

{\bf Z}=\partial_{r}^{2}+(m-1)\,\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{r}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,{\bf Z}^{*}

are not uniquely determined, but are entangled instead. However for the specific (signum)distributions under consideration we expect all actions to be uniquely determined except for the distributions
T_{-m+2}
and
U_{-m+1}
, and the associated signumdistributions
{}^{s}U_{-m+2}
and
{}^{s}T_{-m+1}
.

For general
\lambda
we find:

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}\,T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda(\lambda-1)\,T_{\lambda-2}\quad,\quad\lambda\neq-m+2,-m,-m-2,\ldots

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{r}\,T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\,T_{\lambda-2}\quad,\quad\lambda\neq-m+2,-m,-m-2,\ldots

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,\Delta^{*}\,T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,{\bf{\bf Z}}^{*}\,T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,T_{\lambda-2}\quad,\quad\lambda\neq-m+2

leading to expressions for
\Delta\,T_{\lambda}
and
{\bf Z}\,T_{\lambda}
confirming formulae (
13
) and (
93
) respectively,
and

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}\,U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda(\lambda-1)\,U_{\lambda-2}\quad,\quad\lambda\neq-m+1,-m-1,\ldots

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{r}\,U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\,U_{\lambda-2}\quad,\quad\lambda\neq-m+1,-m-1,\ldots

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,\Delta^{*}\,U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,U_{\lambda-2}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,{\bf{\bf Z}}^{*}\,U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

leading to expressions for
\Delta\,U_{\lambda}
and
{\bf Z}\,U_{\lambda}
confirming formulae (
14
) and (
101
) respectively.
Through signum–pairing we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}\,{}^{s}T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda(\lambda-1)\,^{s}T_{\lambda-2}\quad,\quad\lambda\neq-m+1,-m-1,\ldots

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{r}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\,^{s}T_{\lambda-2}\quad,\quad\lambda\neq-m+1,-m-1,\ldots

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,\Delta^{*}\,{}^{s}T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,{\bf{\bf Z}}^{*}\,{}^{s}T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,^{s}T_{\lambda-2}

leading to expressions for
\Delta\,^{s}T_{\lambda}
and
{\bf Z}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}
confirming formulae (
102
) and (
23
) respectively,
and

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}\,{}^{s}U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda(\lambda-1)\,^{s}U_{\lambda-2}\quad,\quad\lambda\neq-m+2,-m,\ldots

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{r}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\,^{s}U_{\lambda-2}\quad,\quad\lambda\neq-m+2,-m,\ldots

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,\Delta^{*}\,{}^{s}U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,^{s}U_{\lambda-2}\quad,\quad\lambda\neq-m+2

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,{\bf{\bf Z}}^{*}\,{}^{s}U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

leading to expressions for
\Delta\,^{s}U_{\lambda}
and
{\bf Z}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}
confirming formulae (
94
) and (
22
) respectively.

For
\boxed{\lambda=-m+2}
we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}\,T_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m-1)(m-2)\,T_{-m}-(m-2)\,a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{r}\,T_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\,\left(-\,(m-2)\,^{s}T_{-m+1}\right)=-\,(m-2)\,(T_{-m}+\delta(\underline{x})\,c_{3})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,\Delta^{*}\,T_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,{\bf Z}^{*}\,T_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,T_{-m}-(m-1)\,c_{4}\,\delta(\underline{x})

these expressions being entangled by (
19
) and (
95
), viz.

\displaystyle\Delta\,T_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-2)\,a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle{\bf Z}\,T_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,T_{-m}+a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})

These entanglement conditions fix the values of the arbitrary constanst
c_{3}
and
c_{4}
to be
\boxed{c_{3}=0}
and
\boxed{c_{4}=-\,a_{m}}
, eventually leading to

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}\,T_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m-1)(m-2)\,T_{-m}-(m-2)\,a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{r}\,T_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\,\left(-\,(m-2)\,^{s}T_{-m+1}\right)=-\,(m-2)\,T_{-m}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,\Delta^{*}\,T_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,{\bf Z}^{*}\,T_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,T_{-m}+(m-1)\,a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})

For
\boxed{\lambda=-m+1}
we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m(m-1)\,U_{-m-1}+\frac{2m-1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{r}\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,U_{-m-1}-\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,\Delta^{*}\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,U_{-m-1}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,{\bf Z}^{*}\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

confirming the formulae (
18
) and (
103
), viz.

\displaystyle\Delta\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle{\bf Z}\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m-1)\,U_{-m-1}+a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})

Through the signum–pairs of operators
[\partial_{r}^{2},\partial_{r}^{2}]
,
[\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{r},\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{r}]
,
[\frac{1}{r^{2}},\frac{1}{r^{2}}]
and
(\Delta^{*},{\bf Z}^{*})
we then find

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}\,{}^{s}U_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m-1)(m-2)\,^{s}U_{-m}-(m-2)\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{r}\,^{s}U_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-2)\,^{s}U_{-m}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,{\bf Z}^{*}\,U_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,\Delta^{*}\,{}^{s}U_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,^{s}U_{-m}+(m-1)\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})

confirming (
29
) and (

), viz.

\displaystyle{\bf Z}\,^{s}U_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-2)\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle\Delta\,^{s}U_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,^{s}U_{-m}+a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})

and

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}\,{}^{s}T_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m(m-1)\,^{s}T_{-m-1}-\frac{2m-1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{r}\,^{s}T_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,^{s}T_{-m-1}+\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,{\bf Z}^{*}\,{}^{s}T_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,^{s}T_{-m-1}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,\Delta^{*}\,{}^{s}T_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

confirming (
28
) and (
104
), viz.

\displaystyle{\bf Z}\,^{s}T_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle\Delta\,^{s}T_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m-1)\,^{s}T_{-m-1}-a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})

Additionally we also find

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}\,T_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m(m+1)\,T_{-m-2}+\frac{2m+1}{2m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{r}\,T_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,m\,T_{-m-2}-\frac{1}{2m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,\Delta^{*}\,T_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,{\bf Z}^{*}\,T_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,T_{-m-2}

confirming (
17
) and (
97
), viz.

\displaystyle\Delta\,T_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2m\,T_{-m-2}+\frac{m+2}{2m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle{\bf Z}\,T_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+1)\,T_{-m-2}+\frac{m+2}{2m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

and, via signum–pairing,

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}\,{}^{s}U_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m(m+1)\,^{s}U_{-m-2}+\frac{2m+1}{2m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{r}\,^{s}U_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,m\,^{s}U_{-m-2}-\frac{1}{2m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,{\bf Z}^{*}\,U_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,\Delta^{*}\,{}^{s}U_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,^{s}U_{-m-2}

confirming (
27
) and (
98
), viz.

\displaystyle{\bf Z}\,^{s}U_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2m\,^{s}U_{-m-2}+\frac{m+2}{2m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle\Delta\,^{s}U_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+1)\,^{s}U_{-m-2}+\frac{m+2}{2m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

More generally it holds that

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}\,T_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell)(m+2\ell+1)\,T_{-m-2\ell-2}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{(m+2\ell+2)(2m+4\ell+1)}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{r}\,T_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m+2\ell)\,T_{-m-2\ell-2}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{m+2\ell+2}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,\Delta^{*}\,T_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,{\bf Z}^{*}\,T_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,T_{-m-2\ell-2}

confirming (
15
) and (
99
), and

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell)(m+2\ell-1)\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{2m+4\ell-1}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{r}\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m+2\ell-1)\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{1}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,\Delta^{*}\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,U_{-m+2\ell-1}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,{\bf Z}^{*}\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

confirming (
16
) and (

).

Via signum–pairing it also holds that

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}\,{}^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell)(m+2\ell+1)\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell-2}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{(m+2\ell+2)(2m+4\ell+1)}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{r}\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m+2\ell)\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell-2}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{m+2\ell+2}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,{\bf Z}^{*}\,{}^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell-2}

confirming (
24
) and (
100
), and

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}\,{}^{s}T_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell)(m+2\ell-1)\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{2m+4\ell-1}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{r}\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m+2\ell-1)\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{1}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})

Remark 8
.

To give an idea how compuatations with signumdistributions are performed in practice, we will directly prove formula (
92
), viz.

\underline{\partial}\,^{s}T_{-m+1}=-\,(m-1)\,^{s}U_{-m}+a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})

Let
\underline{\omega}\,\varphi\in\Omega(\mathbb{R}^{m};\mathbb{R}^{m})
be a signumdistributional test function, then

\displaystyle\langle\,\underline{\omega}\,\varphi\,,\,\underline{\partial}\,^{s}T_{-m+1}\,\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\langle\,(\underline{\omega}\,\varphi)\,\underline{\partial}\,,\,^{s}T_{-m+1}\,\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\langle\,(1-m)\,\frac{1}{r}\,\varphi+\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}(\varphi)\,,\,^{s}T_{-m+1}\,\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m-1)\,\langle\,\frac{1}{r}\,\varphi\,,\,^{s}T_{-m+1}\,\rangle-\,\langle\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}(\varphi)\,,\,^{s}T_{-m+1}\,\rangle

The first term at the right–hand side equals

(m-1)\,\langle\,\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,\varphi\,,\,U_{-m+1}\,\rangle=(m-1)\,\langle\,\varphi\,,\,\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,U_{-m+1}\,\rangle=(m-1)\,\langle\,\varphi\,,\,T_{-m}\,\rangle=-(m-1)\,\,\langle\,\underline{\omega}\,\varphi\,,\,^{s}U_{-m}\,\rangle

while the second term at the right–hand side equals

\displaystyle-\,a_{m}\,\langle\,\Sigma^{(0)}\left[\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}(\varphi)\right]\,,\,{\rm Fp}\,r_{+}^{0}\,\rangle_{r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,a_{m}\,\langle\,\Sigma^{(1)}\left[\underline{\partial}(\varphi)\right]\,,\,{\rm Fp}\,r_{+}^{0}\,\rangle_{r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\langle\,\underline{\partial}(\varphi)\,,\,U_{-m+1}\,\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\,\varphi\,,\,\underline{\partial}\,U_{-m+1}\,\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,a_{m}\,\langle\,\varphi\,,\,\delta(\underline{x})\,\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{m}\,\langle\,\underline{\omega}\,\varphi\,,\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})\,\rangle

and the formula follows readily.

9
Cartesian derivatives of
T_{\lambda}
and
U_{\lambda}

In this section we compute the first and second order partial derivatives of the distributions
T_{\lambda}
and
U_{\lambda}
and of their associated signumdistributions
{}^{s}T_{\lambda}
and
{}^{s}U_{\lambda}
. Recall that the distributions
T_{\lambda}
are scalar valued, while the distributions
U_{\lambda}
are (Clifford) vector valued with scalar components denoted by
U_{\lambda}^{j},j=1,\ldots,m
. As it holds, for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
, that
\underline{x}\,T_{\lambda}=U_{\lambda+1}
, it follows that these components are given by

U_{\lambda}^{j}=x_{j}\,T_{\lambda-1}\quad,\quad j=1,\ldots,m

Quite naturally the action of the cartesian derivatives
\partial_{x_{j}}
on all of the distributions
T_{\lambda}
and
U_{\lambda}
will be uniquely determined. It follows that the action of the operators
d_{j}
on the associated signumdistributions
{}^{s}U_{\lambda}
and
{}^{s}T_{\lambda}
will be uniquely determined too.

Seen the definition of the operators
d_{j},j=1,\ldots,m
, viz.

d_{j}=\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,e_{j}+(-\,\frac{x_{j}}{r^{2}})+\partial_{x_{j}}=-\,e_{j}\frac{1}{\underline{x}}-(-\,\frac{x_{j}}{r^{2}})+\partial_{x_{j}}

their action on the distributions
T_{\lambda}
and
U_{\lambda}
will be uniquely determined except for the distribution
U_{-m+1}
, and, by signum–pairing, the cartesian derivatives of the associated signumdistributions
{}^{s}U_{\lambda}
and
{}^{s}T_{\lambda}
will be uniquely determined too, except for
{}^{s}T_{-m+1}
.

The actions of the second order cartesian derivatives
\partial_{x_{k}}\partial_{x_{j}}
and
\partial^{2}_{x_{j}}
on the distributions
T_{\lambda}
and
