U_{\lambda}
and of the operators
d_{k}d_{j}
and
d_{j}^{2}
on the associated signumdistributions
{}^{s}U_{\lambda}
and
{}^{s}T_{\lambda}
, will be uniquely determined. Seen the expressions

d_{k}d_{j}=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,(x_{k}+e_{k}\,\underline{x})\,\partial_{x_{j}}-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,\underline{x}\,e_{j}\,\partial_{x_{k}}+\frac{1}{r^{4}}\,x_{k}x_{j}+\frac{1}{r^{4}}\,(\underline{x}\,x_{k}\,e_{j}-e_{k}\,\underline{x}\,x_{k})+\partial_{x_{k}}\partial_{x_{j}}

and

d_{j}^{2}=-\,\frac{1}{r^{2}}-4\,e_{j}\,x_{j}\,\frac{\underline{x}}{r^{4}}-3\,\frac{x_{j}^{2}}{r^{4}}+\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}

the actions of the operators
d_{k}d_{j}
and
d_{j}^{2}
on the distributions
T_{\lambda}
and
U_{\lambda}
will be uniquely determined except for the distributions
T_{-m+2}
and
U_{-m+1}
. Through signum–pairing the actions of the operators
\partial_{x_{k}}\partial_{x_{j}}
and
\partial^{2}_{x_{j}}
on the associated signumdistributions
{}^{s}U_{\lambda}
and
{}^{s}T_{\lambda}
will be uniquely determined too except for
{}^{s}U_{-m+2}
and
{}^{s}T_{-m+1}
.

Notice that

\Sigma_{j<k}\,e_{j}e_{k}\,(d_{j}d_{k}-d_{k}d_{j})=-\,\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,\Gamma

and

\Sigma_{j=1}^{m}\,d_{j}^{2}=\Delta-(m-1)\,\frac{1}{r^{2}}

9.1
First order partial derivatives of
T_{\lambda}

In general, i.e. for
\lambda\neq-m,-m-2,\ldots
, it holds that

\boxed{\partial_{x_{j}}\,T_{\lambda}=\lambda\,x_{j}\,T_{\lambda-2}}

verifying (
7
) by

\underline{\partial}\,T_{\lambda}=\lambda\,\underline{x}\,T_{\lambda-2}=\lambda\,U_{\lambda-1}

and verifying (
30
) by

\mathbb{E}\,T_{\lambda}=\Sigma_{j=1}^{m}\,x_{j}\,\lambda\,x_{j}\,T_{\lambda-2}=-\,\underline{x}^{2}\,T_{\lambda-2}=\lambda\,T_{\lambda}

Through the signumpair of operators
(\partial_{x_{j}},d_{j})
it follows that

d_{j}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}=\lambda\,x_{j}\,^{s}U_{\lambda-2}

Moreover a straightforward computation shows that

\boxed{d_{j}\,T_{\lambda}=-\,U_{\lambda-1}\,e_{j}+(\lambda-1)\,x_{j}\,T_{\lambda-2}=e_{j}\,U_{\lambda-1}+(\lambda+1)\,x_{j}\,T_{\lambda-2}}

whence, through the signumpair of operators
[d_{j},\partial_{x_{j}}]
,

\partial_{x_{j}}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}=\,^{s}T_{\lambda-1}\,e_{j}+(\lambda-1)\,x_{j}\,^{s}U_{\lambda-2}

As a verification we may now compute
\underline{\partial}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}
and we obtain

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-m)\,^{s}T_{\lambda-1}+(\lambda-1)\,\underline{x}\,^{s}U_{\lambda-2}=-\,(\lambda+m-1)\,^{s}T_{\lambda-1}

confirming (
52
).

For the singular values
\lambda=-m,-m-2,\ldots
it holds that

\boxed{\partial_{x_{j}}\,T_{-m-2\ell}=-\,(m+2\ell)\,x_{j}\,T_{-m-2\ell-2}-\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell)}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\,\Delta^{\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})}

with, recall,

C(m,\ell)=2^{2\ell+1}\,\ell!\,\frac{\Gamma(\frac{m}{2}+\ell+1)}{\Gamma(\frac{m}{2})}=2^{\ell}\,\ell!\,m(m+2)(m+4)\cdots(m+2\ell)

verifying (
9
) by

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,T_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m+2\ell)\,\underline{x}\,T_{-m-2\ell-2}-\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell)}\,\underline{\partial}\,\Delta^{\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m+2\ell)\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell)}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

and verifying (
32
) by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\,T_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=-\,(m+2\ell)\,r^{2}\,T_{-m-2\ell-2}-\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell)}\,\mathbb{E}\,\Delta^{\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle=-\,(m+2\ell)\,T_{-m-2\ell}+(-1)^{\ell}\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell)}\,(m+2\ell)\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})

In particular, for
\ell=0
, we have

\partial_{x_{j}}\,T_{-m}=-\,m\,x_{j}\,T_{-m-2}-\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\delta(\underline{x})

verifying (
11
) by

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,T_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,m\,\underline{x}\,T_{-m-2}-\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}\,\delta(\underline{x})=-\,m\,U_{-m-1}-\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}\,\delta(\underline{x})

and verifying (
34
) by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\,T_{-m}

\displaystyle=-\,m\,T_{-m}+a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})

Hence

\displaystyle d_{j}\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m+2\ell)\,x_{j}\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell-2}-\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell)}\,d_{j}\,{\bf Z}^{\ell}\,(\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m+2\ell)\,x_{j}\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell-2}-\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell)}\,\underline{\omega}\,\Delta^{\ell}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\,\delta(\underline{x})

and, in particular, for
\ell=0
,

\displaystyle d_{j}\,^{s}U_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,m\,x_{j}\,^{s}U_{-m-2}-\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,d_{j}\,(\underline{\omega}\delta(\underline{x}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,m\,x_{j}\,^{s}U_{-m-2}-\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\,\delta(\underline{x})

Moreover a direct computation shows that

\boxed{d_{j}\,T_{-m-2\ell}=-\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}\,e_{j}-\,(m+2\ell+1)\,x_{j}\,T_{-m-2\ell-2}-\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell)}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\,\Delta^{\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})}

whence

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{j}}\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\,{}^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-1}\,e_{j}-\,(m+2\ell+1)\,x_{j}\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell-2}-\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell)}\,d_{j}\,{\bf Z}^{\ell}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle=

\,{}^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-1}\,e_{j}-\,(m+2\ell+1)\,x_{j}\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell-2}-\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell)}\,\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\,\Delta^{\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})

and, in particular for
\ell=0
,

d_{j}\,T_{-m}=-\,U_{-m-1}\,e_{j}-\,(m+1)\,x_{j}\,T_{-m-2}-\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\,\delta(\underline{x})

and so

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{j}}\,^{s}U_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\,{}^{s}T_{-m-1}\,e_{j}-\,(m+1)\,x_{j}\,^{s}U_{-m-2}-\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,d_{j}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle=

\,{}^{s}T_{-m-1}\,e_{j}-\,(m+1)\,x_{j}\,^{s}U_{-m-2}-\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\delta(\underline{x})

As a verification of formula (
68
) we now may compute
\underline{\partial}\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}
and we obtain

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-m)\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-1}-(m+2\ell+1)\,\underline{x}\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell-2}-\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell)}\,\Sigma_{\ell=1}^{m}\,e_{j}\,\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\Delta^{\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2\ell+1)\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell)}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})

since, due to the radial character of
\Delta^{\ell}\delta(\underline{x})
, it holds that

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\ell=1}^{m}\,e_{j}\,\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\Delta^{\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\ell=1}^{m}\,e_{j}\,\underline{\omega}\,\omega_{j}\,\partial_{r}\,(-1)^{\ell}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{\ell}\,\Sigma_{\ell=1}^{m}\,e_{j}\,\omega_{j}\,(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r})\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{\ell}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})

9.2
First order partial derivatives of
U_{\lambda}

In general, i.e. for
\lambda\neq-m+1,-m-1,-m-3,\ldots
, it holds that

\boxed{\partial_{x_{j}}\,U_{\lambda}=T_{\lambda-1}\,e_{j}+(\lambda-1)\,x_{j}\,U_{\lambda-2}}

verifying (
8
) by

\underline{\partial}\,U_{\lambda}=-\,m\,T_{\lambda-1}+(\lambda-1)\,\underline{x}\,U_{\lambda-2}=-\,(\lambda+m-1)\,T_{\lambda-1}

verifying and (
31
) by

\mathbb{E}\,U_{\lambda}=\underline{x}\,T_{\lambda-1}+(\lambda-1)\,U_{\lambda}=\lambda\,U_{\lambda}

Through the signumpair of operators
(\partial_{x_{j}},d_{j})
it follows that

d_{j}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}=-\,^{s}U_{\lambda-1}\,e_{j}+(\lambda-1)\,x_{j}\ ^{s}T_{\lambda-2}

Moreover a straightforward computation shows that

\boxed{d_{j}\,U_{\lambda}=\lambda\,x_{j}\,U_{\lambda-2}}

whence, through the signumpair of operators
[d_{j},\partial_{x_{j}}]
,

\partial_{x_{j}}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}=\lambda\,x_{j}\,^{s}T_{\lambda-2}

We may now compute
\underline{\partial}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}
and we obtain

\underline{\partial}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}=\lambda\,\underline{x}\,^{s}T_{\lambda-2}=\lambda\,^{s}U_{\lambda-1}

confirming (
76
).

For the exceptional value
\lambda=-m+1
it holds that

\boxed{\partial_{x_{j}}\,U_{-m+1}=T_{-m}\,e_{j}-\,m\,x_{j}\,U_{-m-1}+\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})\,e_{j}}

verifying (
12
) by

\underline{\partial}\,U_{-m+1}=-\,m\,T_{-m}-m\,\underline{x}\,U_{-m-1}-a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})=-a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})

and verifying (
35
) by

\mathbb{E}\,U_{-m+1}=\underline{x}\,T_{-m}-\,m\,U_{-m+1}+\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{x}\,\delta(\underline{x})=(-m+1)\,U_{-m+1}

Hence

\displaystyle d_{j}\,^{s}T_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,^{s}U_{-m}\,e_{j}-m\,x_{j}\,^{s}T_{-m-1}+\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,x_{j}\,\underline{D}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,^{s}U_{-m}\,e_{j}-m\,x_{j}\,^{s}T_{-m-1}-\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})\,e_{j}

For the action of the operator
d_{j}
we expect a non–uniquely determined result, and, indeed, a direct computation shows that

d_{j}\,U_{-m+1}=-\,(m-1)\,x_{j}\,U_{-m-1}+c^{*}\,x_{j}\,\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})

where we expect the constant
c^{*}
, to be determined by the formulae (
88
) and (
92
).

Indeed we have, through signum–pairing, that

\partial_{x_{j}}\,^{s}T_{-m+1}=-\,(m-1)\,x_{j}\,^{s}T_{-m-1}-c^{*}\,x_{j}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})=-\,(m-1)\,x_{j}\,^{s}T_{-m-1}+c^{*}\,x_{j}\,\partial_{r}\,\delta(\underline{x})

leading to

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,^{s}T_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,\underline{x}\,^{s}T_{-m-1}+c^{*}\,\underline{x}\,\partial_{r}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m-1)\,^{s}U_{-m}-m\,c^{*}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})

However we already found, see (
92
), that

\underline{\partial}\,^{s}T_{-m+1}=-\,(m-1)\,^{s}U_{-m}+a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})

from which it follows that

c^{*}=-\,\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}
,
whence

\boxed{d_{j}\,U_{-m+1}=-\,(m-1)\,x_{j}\,U_{-m-1}-\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,x_{j}\,\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})}

and

\boxed{\partial_{x_{j}}\,^{s}T_{-m+1}=-\,(m-1)\,x_{j}\,^{s}T_{-m-1}+\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,x_{j}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})}

For the singular values
\lambda=-m-1,-m-3,\ldots
it holds that

\boxed{\partial_{x_{j}}\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}=T_{-m-2\ell}\,e_{j}-\,(m+2\ell)\,x_{j}\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell-1}\,\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell)}\,x_{j}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})}

Note that by putting
\ell=0
in the above expression for
\partial_{x_{j}}\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}
, the formula for
\partial_{x_{j}}\,U_{-m+1}
is recovered, and that formula (
10
) is verified through

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,m\,T_{-m-2\ell}+(m+2\ell)\,T_{-m-2\ell}+(-1)^{\ell-1}\,\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell)}\,\underline{x}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\,\ell\,T_{-m-2\ell}+(-1)^{\ell-1}\,(m+2\ell)\,\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell)}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})

Also (
33
) is verified by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\,U_{-m+2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=\underline{x}\,T_{-m-2\ell}-\,(m+2\ell)\,r^{2}\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell)}\,\underline{x}^{2}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle=-\,(m+2\ell-1)\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell)}\,(m+2\ell)(2\ell)\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell-1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle=-\,(m+2\ell-1)\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell-1)}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell-1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

Through signum–pairing we obtain

\displaystyle d_{j}\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}\,e_{j}-(m+2\ell)\,x_{j}\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell)}\,x_{j}\,\underline{D}^{2\ell+1}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}\,e_{j}-(m+2\ell)\,x_{j}\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell)}\,\underline{\omega}\,x_{j}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

Moreover a direct computation leads to

\displaystyle\boxed{d_{j}\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}=-\,(m+2\ell-1)\,x_{j}\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{l-1}\,\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell)}\,x_{j}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})}

Note that by putting
\ell=0
in the above expression for
d_{j}\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}
, the formula for
d_{j}\,U_{-m+1}
is recovered. Through signum–pairing we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{j}}\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-\,(m+2\ell-1)\,x_{j}\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{l}\,\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell)}\,x_{j}\,\underline{D}^{2\ell+1}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-\,(m+2\ell-1)\,x_{j}\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{l}\,\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell)}\,\underline{\omega}\,x_{j}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

We may now verify formula (
84
) by

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m+2\ell-1)\,\underline{x}\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell)}\,\Sigma_{j=1}^{m}\,e_{j}\,\underline{\omega}\,x_{j}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})

which indeed reduces to

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,(m+2\ell-1)\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell)}\,(m+2\ell)\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\delta(\underline{x})

since

\displaystyle\Sigma_{j=1}^{\ell}\,e_{j}\,\underline{\omega}\,x_{j}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Sigma_{j=1}^{m}\,e_{j}\,\underline{\omega}\,r\,\omega_{j}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,r\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})=\underline{\omega}\,(r\partial_{r})\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell)\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\delta(\underline{x})

9.3
Second order partial derivatives of
T_{\lambda}

First we assume that
\lambda\neq-m+2,-m,-m-2,\ldots
, and we find

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{k}}\partial_{x_{j}}\,T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\,(\lambda-2)\,x_{k}\,x_{j}\,T_{\lambda-4}\quad,\quad j\neq k

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}\,T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\,(\lambda-2)\,x_{j}^{2}\,T_{\lambda-4}+\lambda\,T_{\lambda-2}

confirming (
13
) through

\Delta\,T_{\lambda}=\lambda\,(\lambda-2)\,r^{2}\,T_{\lambda-4}+m\,\lambda\,T_{\lambda-2}=\lambda\,(\lambda+m-2)\,T_{\lambda-2}

Through the signumpartners of
\partial_{x_{k}}
,
\partial_{x_{j}}
and
T_{\lambda}
, it follows that

\displaystyle d_{k}\,d_{j}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\,(\lambda-2)\,x_{k}\,x_{j}\,^{s}U_{\lambda-4}\quad,\quad j\neq k

\displaystyle d_{j}^{2}\,{}^{s}U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\,(\lambda-2)\,x_{j}^{2}\,{}^{s}U_{\lambda-4}+\lambda^{s}U_{\lambda-2}

A direct computation shows that

\displaystyle d_{k}d_{j}\,T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\lambda-1)(\lambda-3)\,x_{k}\,x_{j}\,T_{\lambda-4}-(\lambda-1)\,U_{\lambda-3}\,(x_{k}\,e_{j}+x_{j}\,e_{k})\quad,\quad j\neq k

and

\displaystyle d_{j}^{2}\,T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\lambda-1)(\lambda-3)\,x_{j}^{2}\,T_{\lambda-4}-2(\lambda-1)\,U_{\lambda-3}\,x_{j}\,e_{j}+(\lambda-1)\,T_{\lambda-2}

from which it follows at once that

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{k}}\partial_{x_{j}}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\lambda-1)(\lambda-3)\,x_{k}\,x_{j}\,^{s}U_{\lambda-4}+(\lambda-1)\,^{s}T_{\lambda-3}\,(x_{k}\,e_{j}+x_{j}\,e_{k})\quad,\quad j\neq k

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}\,{}^{s}U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\lambda-1)(\lambda-3)\,x_{j}^{2}\,{}^{s}U_{\lambda-4}+2(\lambda-1)\,^{s}T_{\lambda-3}\,x_{j}\,e_{j}+(\lambda-1)\,^{s}U_{\lambda-2}

confirming (
94
) through

\Delta\,^{s}U_{\lambda}=(\lambda-1)(\lambda-3)\,r^{2}\,{}^{s}U_{\lambda-4}+2(\lambda-1)\,^{s}T_{\lambda-3}\,\underline{x}+m\,(\lambda-1)\,^{s}U_{\lambda-2}=(\lambda-1)\,(\lambda+m-1)\,^{s}U_{\lambda-2}

For the singular values of the parameter
\lambda
, viz.
\lambda=-m+2,-m,-m-2,\ldots
, specific computations are necessary.

For
\lambda=-m+2
we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{k}}\partial_{x_{j}}\,T_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m\,(m-2)\,x_{k}\,x_{j}\,T_{-m-2}\quad,\quad j\neq k

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}\,T_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m\,(m-2)\,x_{j}^{2}\,T_{-m-2}-(m-2)\,T_{-m}-\frac{m-2}{m}\,a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})

confirming (
19
) through

\Delta\,T_{-m+2}=m\,(m-2)\,r^{2}\,T_{-m-2}-m(m-2)\,T_{-m}-(m-2)\,a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})=-(m-2)\,a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})

It follows that

\displaystyle d_{k}d_{j}\,^{s}U_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m\,(m-2)\,x_{k}\,x_{j}\,^{s}U_{-m-2}\quad,\quad j\neq k

\displaystyle d_{j}^{2}\,{}^{s}U_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m\,(m-2)\,x_{j}^{2}\,{}^{s}U_{-m-2}-(m-2)\,^{s}U_{-m}-\frac{m-2}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})

A direct computation shows that

\displaystyle d_{k}d_{j}\,T_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m-1)(m+1)\,x_{j}\,x_{k}\,T_{-m-2}+(m-1)\,U_{-m-1}\,(x_{k}\,e_{j}+x_{j}\,e_{k})-\alpha_{k}\,\delta(\underline{x})\,e_{k}\,e_{j}\quad,\quad j\neq k

\displaystyle d_{j}^{2}\,T_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m-1)(m+1)\,x_{j}^{2}\,T_{-m-2}+2\,(m-1)\,U_{-m-1}\,x_{j}\,e_{j}+(-m+1)\,T_{-m}+(\alpha_{j}-\frac{m-1}{m}\,a_{m})\,\delta(\underline{x})

whence also

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{k}}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\,^{s}U_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m-1)(m+1)\,x_{j}\,x_{k}\,^{s}U_{-m-2}+(-m+1)\,^{s}T_{-m-1}\,(x_{k}\,e_{j}+x_{j}\,e_{k})-\alpha_{k}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})\,e_{k}\,e_{j}\quad,\quad j\neq k

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}\,{}^{s}U_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m-1)(m+1)\,x_{j}^{2}\,{}^{s}U_{-m-2}+2\,(-m+1)\,^{s}T_{-m-1}\,x_{j}\,e_{j}+(-m+1)\,^{s}U_{-m}+(\alpha_{j}-\frac{m-1}{m}\,a_{m})\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})

leading to

\Delta\,^{s}U_{-m+2}=-\,(m-1)\,^{s}U_{-m}+((\Sigma\,\alpha_{j})-(m-1)\,a_{m})\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})

But we already found, see (
96
), that

\Delta\,^{s}U_{-m+2}=-\,(m-1)\,^{s}U_{-m}+a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})

whence all
\alpha_{j},j=1,\ldots,m
must be equal to
a_{m}
, which eventually leads to

\displaystyle d_{k}d_{j}\,T_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m-1)(m+1)\,x_{j}\,x_{k}\,T_{-m-2}+(m-1)\,U_{-m-1}\,(x_{k}\,e_{j}+x_{j}\,e_{k})-a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})\,e_{k}\,e_{j}\quad,\quad j\neq k

\displaystyle d_{j}^{2}\,T_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m-1)(m+1)\,x_{j}^{2}\,T_{-m-2}+2\,(m-1)\,U_{-m-1}\,x_{j}\,e_{j}+(-m+1)\,T_{-m}+\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})

and

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{k}}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\,^{s}U_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m-1)(m+1)\,x_{j}\,x_{k}\,^{s}U_{-m-2}+(-m+1)\,^{s}T_{-m-1}\,(x_{k}\,e_{j}+x_{j}\,e_{k})-a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})\,e_{k}\,e_{j}\quad,\quad j\neq k

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}\,{}^{s}U_{-m+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m-1)(m+1)\,x_{j}^{2}\,{}^{s}U_{-m-2}+2\,(-m+1)\,^{s}T_{-m-1}\,x_{j}\,e_{j}+(-m+1)\,^{s}U_{-m}+\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})

For
\lambda=-m-2\ell,\ell=0,1,2,\ldots
we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{k}}\partial_{x_{j}}\,T_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell)(m+2\ell+2)\,x_{k}\,x_{j}\,T_{-m-2\ell-4}-\,\frac{2m+4\ell+2}{(m+2\ell+2)C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\partial_{x_{k}}\partial_{x_{j}}\,\Delta^{\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})\quad,\quad j\neq k

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}\,T_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell)(m+2\ell+2)\,x_{j}^{2}\,T_{-m-2\ell-4}-\,\frac{2m+4\ell+2}{(m+2\ell+2)C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}\,\Delta^{\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})-\,(m+2\ell)\,T_{-m-2\ell-2}-\,\frac{m+2\ell}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\Delta^{\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

confirming (
15
) through

\displaystyle\Delta\,T_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=(m+2\ell)(m+2\ell+2)\,T_{-m-2\ell-2}-\,\frac{2m+4\ell+2}{(m+2\ell+2)C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\Delta^{\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})-\,m\,(m+2\ell)\,T_{-m-2\ell-2}-\,\frac{m(m+2\ell)}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\Delta^{\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle=(m+2\ell)(2\ell+2)\,T_{-m-2\ell-2}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{(m+4\ell+2)(m+2\ell+2)}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

It follows that

\displaystyle d_{k}\,d_{j}\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell)(m+2\ell+2)\,x_{k}\,x_{j}\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell-4}-\,\frac{2m+4\ell+2}{(m+2\ell+2)C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{x_{k}}\partial_{x_{j}}\,\Delta^{\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})\quad,\quad j\neq k

\displaystyle d_{j}^{2}\,{}^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell)(m+2\ell+2)\,x_{j}^{2}\,{}^{s}U_{-m-2\ell-4}-\,\frac{2m+4\ell+2}{(m+2\ell+2)C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}\,\Delta^{\ell}\,\delta(\underline{x})-\,(m+2\ell)\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell-2}-\,\frac{m+2\ell}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\Delta^{\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

In particular, for
\lambda=-m
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{k}}\partial_{x_{j}}\,T_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m(m+2)\,x_{k}\,x_{j}\,T_{-m-4}-\,\frac{2m+2}{m(m+2)}\,a_{m}\,\partial_{x_{k}}\partial_{x_{j}}\,\delta(\underline{x})\quad,\quad j\neq k

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}\,T_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m(m+2)\,x_{j}^{2}\,T_{-m-4}-\,\frac{2m+2}{m(m+2)}\,a_{m}\,\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}\,\delta(\underline{x})-m\,T_{-m-2}-\,\frac{1}{2(m+2)}\,a_{m}\,\Delta\,\delta(\underline{x})

confirming (
17
) through

\displaystyle\Delta\,T_{-m}

\displaystyle=m(m+2)\,T_{-m-2}-\,\frac{2m+2}{m(m+2)}\,a_{m}\,\Delta\,\delta(\underline{x})-m^{2}\,T_{-m-2}-\,\frac{m}{2(m+2)}\,a_{m}\,\Delta\,\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle=2\,m\,T_{-m}-\frac{m+2}{m}\,a_{m}\,\Delta\,\delta(\underline{x})

It follows that

\displaystyle d_{k}\,d_{j}\,^{s}U_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m(m+2)\,x_{k}\,x_{j}\,^{s}U_{-m-4}-\,\frac{2m+2}{m(m+2)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{x_{k}}\partial_{x_{j}}\,\delta(\underline{x})\quad,\quad j\neq k

\displaystyle d_{j}^{2}\,{}^{s}U_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m(m+2)\,x_{j}^{2}\,{}^{s}U_{-m-4}-\,\frac{2m+2}{m(m+2)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}\,\delta(\underline{x})-m\,^{s}U_{-m-2}-\,\frac{1}{2(m+2)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\Delta\,\delta(\underline{x})

A direct computation shows that

\displaystyle d_{k}\,d_{j}\,T_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell+1)\,U_{-m-2\ell-3}\,(x_{k}\,e_{j}+x_{j}\,e_{k})+(m+2\ell+1)(m+2\ell+3)\,x_{j}\,x_{k}\,T_{-m-2\ell-4}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{1}{(m+2\ell+2)C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,(\partial_{x_{k}}\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})\,e_{j}+\partial_{x_{j}}\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})\,e_{k})+2(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell)}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\partial_{x_{k}}\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\delta(\underline{x})\quad,\quad j\neq k

\displaystyle d_{j}^{2}\,T_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2(m+2\ell+1)\,U_{-m-2\ell-3}\,x_{j}\,e_{j}-(m+2\ell+1)\,T_{-m-2\ell-2}+(m+2\ell+1)(m+2\ell+3)\,x_{j}^{2}\,T_{-m-2\ell-4}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{m+2\ell}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+2}\delta(\underline{x})+2(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{1}{(m+2\ell+2)C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})\,e_{j}+2(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{1}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\delta(\underline{x})

whence also

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{k}}\partial_{x_{j}}\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(m+2\ell+1)\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-3}\,(x_{k}\,e_{j}+x_{j}\,e_{k})+(m+2\ell+1)(m+2\ell+3)\,x_{j}\,x_{k}\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell-4}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{1}{(m+2\ell+2)C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,(\partial_{x_{k}}\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})\,e_{j}+\partial_{x_{j}}\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})\,e_{k})+2(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell)}\,\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\partial_{x_{k}}\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\delta(\underline{x})\quad,\quad j\neq k

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}\,{}^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2(m+2\ell+1)\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-3}\,x_{j}\,e_{j}-(m+2\ell+1)\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell-2}+(m+2\ell+1)(m+2\ell+3)\,x_{j}^{2}\,{}^{s}U_{-m-2\ell-4}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{m+2\ell}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+2}\delta(\underline{x})+2(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{1}{(m+2\ell+2)C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})\,e_{j}+2(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{1}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\delta(\underline{x})

confirming (
100
) through

\Delta\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}=(m+2\ell+1)(2\ell+1)\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell-2}+(-1)^{\ell}\,(m+2\ell+2)(m+4\ell+2)\,\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+2}\delta(\underline{x})

In particular, for
\lambda=-m
, we have

\displaystyle d_{k}\,d_{j}\,\,T_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+1)\,U_{-m-3}\,(x_{k}\,e_{j}+x_{j}\,e_{k})+(m+1)(m+3)\,x_{j}\,x_{k}\,T_{-m-4}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m(m+2)}\,a_{m}\,(\partial_{x_{k}}\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})\,e_{j}+\partial_{x_{j}}\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})\,e_{k})-\frac{2}{m}\,a_{m}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\partial_{x_{k}}\delta(\underline{x})\quad,\quad j\neq k

\displaystyle d_{j}^{2}\,T_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2(m+1)\,U_{-m-3}\,x_{j}\,e_{j}-(m+1)\,T_{-m-2}+(m+1)(m+3)\,x_{j}^{2}\,T_{-m-4}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2(m+2)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2}\delta(\underline{x})-2\,\frac{1}{m(m+2)}\,a_{m}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\,\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})\,e_{j}-2\,\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

whence also

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{k}}\partial_{x_{j}}\,^{s}U_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(m+1)\,^{s}T_{-m-3}\,(x_{k}\,e_{j}+x_{j}\,e_{k})+(m+1)(m+3)\,x_{j}\,x_{k}\,^{s}U_{-m-4}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m(m+2)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,(\partial_{x_{k}}\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})\,e_{j}+\partial_{x_{j}}\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})\,e_{k})-\frac{2}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\partial_{x_{k}}\delta(\underline{x})\quad,\quad j\neq k

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}\,{}^{s}U_{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2(m+1)\,^{s}T_{-m-3}\,x_{j}\,e_{j}-(m+1)\,^{s}U_{-m-2}+(m+1)(m+3)\,x_{j}^{2}\,{}^{s}U_{-m-4}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2(m+2)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2}\delta(\underline{x})-2\,\frac{1}{m(m+2)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\,\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})\,e_{j}-2\,\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

which confirms (
98
).

9.4
Second order partial derivatives of
U_{\lambda}

First we compute
\partial_{x_{k}}\partial_{x_{j}}\,U_{\lambda}
assuming that
\lambda\neq-m+3,-m+1,-m-1,\ldots
, and we find

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{k}}\partial_{x_{j}}\,U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\lambda-1)(\lambda-3)\,x_{k}\,x_{j}\,U_{\lambda-4}+(\lambda-1)\,T_{\lambda-3}\,(x_{k}\,e_{j}+x_{j}\,e_{k})\quad,\quad j\neq k

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}\,U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\lambda-1)(\lambda-3)\,x_{j}^{2}\,U_{\lambda-4}+2(\lambda-1)\,T_{\lambda-3}\,x_{j}\,e_{j}+(\lambda-1)\,U_{\lambda-2}

confirming (
14
) through

\displaystyle\Delta\,U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=(\lambda-1)(\lambda-3)\,U_{\lambda-2}+2(\lambda-1)\,T_{\lambda-3}\,\underline{x}+m(\lambda-1)\,U_{\lambda-2}

\displaystyle=(\lambda-1)(\lambda+m-1)\,U_{\lambda-2}

It follows at once that

\displaystyle d_{k}\,d_{j}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\lambda-1)(\lambda-3)\,x_{k}\,x_{j}\,^{s}T_{\lambda-4}-(\lambda-1)\,^{s}U_{\lambda-3}\,(x_{k}\,e_{j}+x_{j}\,e_{k})\quad,\quad j\neq k

\displaystyle d_{j}^{2}\,{}^{s}T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\lambda-1)(\lambda-3)\,x_{j}^{2}\,{}^{s}T_{\lambda-4}-2(\lambda-1)\,^{s}U_{\lambda-3}\,x_{j}\,e_{j}+(\lambda-1)\,^{s}T_{\lambda-2}

A direct computation shows that

\displaystyle d_{k}\,d_{j}\,U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\,(\lambda-2)\,x_{k}\,x_{j}\,U_{\lambda-4}\quad,\quad j\neq k

\displaystyle d_{j}^{2}\,U_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\,(\lambda-2)\,x_{j}^{2}\,U_{\lambda-4}+\lambda\,U_{\lambda-2}

from which it follows that

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{k}}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\,(\lambda-2)\,x_{k}\,x_{j}\,^{s}T_{\lambda-4}\quad,\quad j\neq k

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}\,{}^{s}T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\,(\lambda-2)\,x_{j}^{2}\,{}^{s}T_{\lambda-4}+\lambda\,^{s}T_{\lambda-2}

confirming (
102
) through

\displaystyle\Delta\,^{s}T_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\lambda\,(\lambda-2)\,^{s}T_{\lambda-2}+m\,\lambda\,^{s}T_{\lambda-2}

\displaystyle=\lambda(\lambda+m-2)\,^{s}T_{\lambda-2}

For
\lambda=-m-2\ell+1,\ell=1,2,\ldots
we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{k}}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(m+2\ell)\,T_{-m-2\ell-2}\,(x_{k}\,e_{j}+x_{j}\,e_{k})+(m+2\ell)(m+2\ell+2)\,x_{j}\,x_{k}\,U_{-m-2\ell-3}

\displaystyle+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{m+2\ell}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,x_{j}\,x_{k}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+3}\delta(\underline{x})+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,(2\ell)\,\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell)}\,\partial_{x_{k}}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell-1}\delta(\underline{x})\quad,\quad j\neq k

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2(m+2\ell)\,x_{j}\,e_{j}\,T_{-m-2\ell-2}-(m+2\ell)\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}+(m+2\ell)(m+2\ell+2)\,x_{j}^{2}\,U_{-m-2\ell-3}

\displaystyle+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{m+2\ell}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,x_{j}^{2}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+3}\delta(\underline{x})+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{2\ell}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell-1}\delta(\underline{x})

confirming (
16
) through

\displaystyle\Delta\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=-2(m+2\ell)\,\underline{x}\,T_{-m-2\ell-2}-m(m+2\ell)\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}+(m+2\ell)(m+2\ell+2)\,U_{-m-2\ell-1)}

\displaystyle\phantom{m+2\ell}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{m+2\ell}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{x}^{2}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+3}\delta(\underline{x})+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{2\ell}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle=(m+2\ell)(2\ell)\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{m+4\ell}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})

Hence also

\displaystyle d_{k}\,d_{j}\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell)\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell-2}\,(x_{k}\,e_{j}+x_{j}\,e_{k})+(m+2\ell)(m+2\ell+2)\,x_{j}\,x_{k}\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-3}

\displaystyle+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{m+2\ell}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,x_{j}\,x_{k}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+3}\delta(\underline{x})+(-1)^{\ell}\,(2\ell)\,\frac{a_{m}}{C(m,\ell)}\,\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{x_{k}}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell-1}\delta(\underline{x})\quad,\quad j\neq k

\displaystyle d_{j}^{2}\,{}^{s}T_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2(m+2\ell)\,x_{j}\,e_{j}\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell-2}-(m+2\ell)\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-1}+(m+2\ell)(m+2\ell+2)\,x_{j}^{2}\,{}^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-3}

\displaystyle+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{m+2\ell}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,x_{j}^{2}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+3}\delta(\underline{x})+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{2\ell}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell-1}\delta(\underline{x})

A direct computation shows that

\displaystyle d_{k}d_{j}\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell-1)(m+2\ell+1)\,x_{j}\,x_{k}\,U_{-m-2\ell-3}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{2(m+2\ell)(2\ell+2)}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,(e_{k}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\delta(\underline{x})+e_{j}\,\partial_{x_{k}}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\delta(\underline{x}))

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{2(m+2\ell)(2\ell+2)(2\ell)}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\,\partial_{x_{k}}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell-1}\delta(\underline{x})\quad,\quad j\neq k

\displaystyle d_{j}^{2}\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell-1)(m+2\ell+1)\,x_{j}^{2}\,U_{-m-2\ell-3}-(m+2\ell-1)\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{4(m+2\ell)(2\ell+2)}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,e_{j}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{(2\ell+2)(2\ell)(2m+4\ell)}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell-1}\delta(\underline{x})+(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{(2\ell+2)(m+2\ell-2)}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})

whence also

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{k}}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell-1)(m+2\ell+1)\,x_{j}\,x_{k}\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-3}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{2(m+2\ell)(2\ell+2)}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,(e_{k}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\delta(\underline{x})+e_{j}\,\partial_{x_{k}}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\delta(\underline{x}))

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{2(m+2\ell)(2\ell+2)(2\ell)}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\,\partial_{x_{k}}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell-1}\delta(\underline{x})\quad,\quad j\neq k

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}\,{}^{s}T_{-m-2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+2\ell-1)(m+2\ell+1)\,x_{j}^{2}\,{}^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-3}-(m+2\ell-1)\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{4(m+2\ell)(2\ell+2)}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,e_{j}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{(2\ell+2)(2\ell)(2m+4\ell)}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell-1}\delta(\underline{x})+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{(2\ell+2)(m+2\ell-2)}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})

which confirms (
106
).

For
\lambda=-m+1
, it holds that

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{k}}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-m)\,T_{-m-2}\,(x_{k}\,e_{j}+x_{j}\,e_{k})+m(m+2)\,x_{j}\,x_{k}\,U_{-m-3}-\frac{a_{m}}{2(m+2)}\,x_{j}\,x_{k}\,\underline{\partial}^{3}\delta(\underline{x})\quad,\quad j\neq k

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2(-m)\,x_{j}\,e_{j}\,T_{-m-2}-m\,U_{-m-1}+m(m+2)\,x_{j}^{2}\,U_{-m-3}-\frac{a_{m}}{2(m+2)}\,x_{j}^{2}\,\underline{\partial}^{3}\delta(\underline{x})

confirming (
18
) through

\displaystyle\Delta\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=2(-m)\,\underline{x}\,T_{-m-2}-m^{2}\,U_{-m-1}+m(m+2)\,r^{2}\,U_{-m-3}-\frac{1}{2(m+2)}\,a_{m}\,r^{2}\,\underline{\partial}^{3}\delta(\underline{x})

\displaystyle=a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})

Hence also

\displaystyle d_{k}\,d_{j}\,^{s}T_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m\,^{s}U_{-m-2}\,(x_{k}\,e_{j}+x_{j}\,e_{k})+m(m+2)\,x_{j}\,x_{k}\,^{s}T_{-m-3}+\frac{a_{m}}{2(m+2)}\,x_{j}\,x_{k}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{3}\delta(\underline{x})\quad,\quad j\neq k

\displaystyle d_{j}^{2}\,{}^{s}T_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\,m\,x_{j}\,e_{j}\,^{s}U_{-m-2}-m\,^{s}T_{-m-1}+m(m+2)\,x_{j}^{2}\,{}^{s}T_{-m-3}+\frac{a_{m}}{2(m+2)}\,x_{j}^{2}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{3}\delta(\underline{x})

We know that a direct computation of
d_{k}\,d_{j}\,U_{-m+1}
and
d_{j}^{2}\,U_{-m+1}
will involve arbitrary constants to be determined through the calculation of
\Delta\,^{s}T_{-m+1}
. We can circumvent these computations by putting
\ell=0
in the expressions for

d_{k}\,d_{j}\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}
and
d_{j}^{2}\,U_{-m-2\ell+1}
obtaining in this way:

\displaystyle d_{k}\,d_{j}\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m-1)(m+1)\,x_{k}\,x_{j}\,U_{-m-3}+\frac{2}{m+2}\,a_{m}\,(e_{k}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\delta(\underline{x})+e_{j}\,\partial_{x_{k}}\delta(\underline{x}))\quad,\quad j\neq k

\displaystyle d_{j}^{2}\,U_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m-1)(m+1)\,xj^{2}\,U_{-m-3}-(m-1)\,U_{-m-1}+\frac{4}{m+2}\,a_{m}\,e_{j}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\delta(\underline{x})+\frac{m-2}{m(m+2)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})

whence also

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{k}}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\,^{s}T_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m-1)(m+1)\,x_{k}\,x_{j}\,^{s}T_{-m-3}-\frac{2}{m+2}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,(e_{k}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\delta(\underline{x})+e_{j}\,\partial_{x_{k}}\delta(\underline{x}))\quad,\quad j\neq k

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}\,{}^{s}T_{-m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m-1)(m+1)\,xj^{2}\,{}^{s}T_{-m-3}-(m-1)\,^{s}T_{-m-1}-\frac{4}{m+2}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,e_{j}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\delta(\underline{x})-\frac{m-2}{m(m+2)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})

which indeed confirms (
104
).

10
Powers of the vector variable
\underline{x}

In this section we will explore the intimate connection between the
T_{\lambda}
and
U_{\lambda}
distributions on the one hand and powers of the Clifford vector variable
\underline{x}
on the other.

10.1
Integer powers of
\underline{x}
as regular (signum)distributions

The integer powers
\underline{x}^{k},k\in\mathbb{Z}
, of the vector variable
\underline{x}
remain locally integrable in
\mathbb{R}^{m}
as long as
k\geq-m+1
, and so may be interpreted as regular distributions, viz. for all test functions
\varphi(\underline{x})\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{m};\mathbb{C})
one has

\langle\ \underline{x}^{k}\ ,\ \varphi(\underline{x})\ \rangle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}\,\underline{x}^{k}\,\varphi(\underline{x})\,d\underline{x}\quad,\quad k\geq-m+1

In particular for even integer powers:
k=2\ell\geq-m+1
we have

\displaystyle\langle\ \underline{x}^{2\ell}\ ,\ \varphi(\underline{x})\ \rangle\

\displaystyle=\ \int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell}\,\varphi(\underline{x})\,d\underline{x}\ =\ (-1)^{\ell}\,\int_{0}^{+\infty}\,r^{m+2\ell-1}\,dr\,\int_{S^{m-1}}\,\varphi(r\underline{\omega})\,dS_{\underline{\omega}}

\displaystyle=\ (-1)^{\ell}\,a_{m}\,\int_{0}^{+\infty}\,r^{m+2\ell-1}\,\Sigma^{(0)}[\varphi]\,dr\ =\ (-1)^{\ell}\,a_{m}\,\langle\ r_{+}^{m+2\ell-1}\ ,\ \Sigma^{(0)}[\varphi]\ \rangle

implying that

\boxed{\underline{x}^{2\ell}=(-1)^{\ell}\,T_{2\ell}\quad,\quad 2\ell\geq-m+1}

which clearly are radial regular distributions.

Similarly, for odd integer powers
k=2\ell+1\geq-m+1
we find

\displaystyle\langle\ \underline{x}^{2\ell+1}\ ,\ \varphi(\underline{x})\ \rangle\

\displaystyle=\ \int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell+1}\,\varphi(\underline{x})\,d\underline{x}\ =\ (-1)^{\ell}\,\int_{0}^{+\infty}\,r^{m+2\ell}\,dr\,\int_{S^{m-1}}\,\underline{\omega}\,\varphi(r\underline{\omega})\,dS_{\underline{\omega}}

\displaystyle=\ (-1)^{\ell}\,a_{m}\,\int_{0}^{+\infty}\,r^{m+2\ell}\,\Sigma^{(1)}[\varphi]\,dr\ =\ (-1)^{\ell}\,a_{m}\,\langle\ r_{+}^{m+2\ell}\ ,\ \Sigma^{(1)}[\varphi]\ \rangle

implying that

\boxed{\underline{x}^{2\ell+1}=(-1)^{\ell}\,U_{2\ell+1}\quad,\quad 2\ell\geq-m}

which clearly are signum–radial regular distributions.

The associated signumdistributions of these integer powers of
\underline{x}
are:

(\underline{x}^{2\ell})^{\vee}=\underline{\omega}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell}=(-1)^{\ell}\,{}^{s}U_{2\ell}

and

(\underline{x}^{2\ell+1})^{\vee}=\underline{\omega}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell+1}=(-1)^{\ell+1}\,{}^{s}T_{2\ell+1}

but these signumdistributions are by no means powers of the vector variable
\underline{x}
.

As to derivation we have

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell}

\displaystyle=-\,(2\ell)\,\underline{x}^{2\ell-1}\qquad

\displaystyle\underline{D}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell}

\displaystyle=-\,(m+2\ell-1)\,\underline{x}^{2\ell-1}\quad

\displaystyle,\quad

\displaystyle 2\ell-1\geq-m+1

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=-\,(m+2\ell)\,\underline{x}^{2\ell}\qquad

\displaystyle\underline{D}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=-\,(2\ell+1)\,\underline{x}^{2\ell}\quad

\displaystyle,\quad

\displaystyle 2\ell\geq-m+1

and the corresponding formulae for the associated signumdistributions:

\displaystyle\underline{D}\,(\underline{\omega}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell})

\displaystyle=-\,(2\ell)\,(\underline{\omega}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell-1})\qquad

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,(\underline{\omega}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell})

\displaystyle=-\,(m+2\ell-1)\,(\underline{\omega}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell-1})\quad

\displaystyle,\quad

\displaystyle 2\ell-1\geq-m+1

\displaystyle\underline{D}\,(\underline{\omega}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell+1})

\displaystyle=-\,(m+2\ell)\,(\underline{\omega}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell})\qquad

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,(\underline{\omega}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell+1})

\displaystyle=-\,(2\ell+1)\,(\underline{\omega}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell})\quad

\displaystyle,\quad

\displaystyle 2\ell\geq-m+1

We know that locally integrable functions may serve as regular signumdistributions as well: for all test functions
\underline{\omega}\,\varphi(\underline{x})\in\Omega(\mathbb{R}^{m};\mathbb{C})
one has

\langle\ ^{s}\underline{x}^{k}\ ,\ \underline{\omega}\,\varphi(\underline{x})\ \rangle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}\,\underline{x}^{k}\,\underline{\omega}\,\varphi(\underline{x})\,d\underline{x}\quad,\quad k\geq-m+1

In particular for even integer powers:
k=2\ell\geq-m+1
we have

\displaystyle\langle\ ^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell}\ ,\ \underline{\omega}\,\varphi(\underline{x})\ \rangle\

\displaystyle=\ \int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell}\,\underline{\omega}\,\varphi(\underline{x})\,d\underline{x}\ =\ (-1)^{\ell}\,\int_{0}^{+\infty}\,r^{m+2\ell-1}\,dr\,\int_{S^{m-1}}\,\underline{\omega}\,\varphi(r\underline{\omega})\,dS_{\underline{\omega}}

\displaystyle=\ (-1)^{\ell}\,a_{m}\,\int_{0}^{+\infty}\,r^{m+2\ell-1}\,\Sigma^{(1)}[\varphi]\,dr\ =\ (-1)^{\ell}\,a_{m}\,\langle\ r_{+}^{m+2\ell-1}\ ,\ \Sigma^{(1)}[\varphi]\ \rangle

implying that

\boxed{{}^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell}=(-1)^{\ell}\,{}^{s}T_{2\ell}\quad,\quad 2\ell\geq-m+1}

which clearly are radial regular signumdistributions.

Similarly, for odd integer powers
k=2\ell+1\geq-m+1
we find

\displaystyle\langle\ ^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell+1}\ ,\ \underline{\omega}\,\varphi(\underline{x})\ \rangle\

\displaystyle=\ \int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell+1}\,\underline{\omega}\,\varphi(\underline{x})\,d\underline{x}\ =\ (-1)^{\ell+1}\,\int_{0}^{+\infty}\,r^{m+2\ell}\,dr\,\int_{S^{m-1}}\,\varphi(r\underline{\omega})\,dS_{\underline{\omega}}

\displaystyle=\ (-1)^{\ell+1}\,a_{m}\,\int_{0}^{+\infty}\,r^{m+2\ell}\,\Sigma^{(0)}[\varphi]\,dr\ =\ (-1)^{\ell+1}\,a_{m}\,\langle\ r_{+}^{m+2\ell}\ ,\ \Sigma^{(0)}[\varphi]\ \rangle

implying that

\boxed{{}^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell+1}=(-1)^{\ell}\,{}^{s}U_{2\ell+1}\quad,\quad 2\ell\geq-m}

which clearly signum–radial regular signumdistributions.

The associated distributions of these integer power signumdistributions are:

(^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell})^{\wedge}=-\,\underline{\omega}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell}=(-1)^{\ell+1}\,U_{2\ell}

and

(^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell+1})^{\wedge}=-\,\underline{\omega}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell+1}=(-1)^{\ell}\,T_{2\ell+1}

but these distributions are by no means powers of the vector variable
\underline{x}
.

As to derivation we have

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell}

\displaystyle=-\,(2\ell)\,^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell-1}\qquad

\displaystyle\underline{D}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell}

\displaystyle=-\,(m+2\ell-1)\,^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell-1}\quad

\displaystyle,\quad

\displaystyle 2\ell-1\geq-m+1

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=-\,(m+2\ell)\,^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell}\qquad

\displaystyle\underline{D}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=-\,(2\ell+1)\,^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell}\quad

\displaystyle,\quad

\displaystyle 2\ell\geq-m+1

and the corresponding formulae for the associated distributions:

\displaystyle\underline{D}\,(\underline{\omega}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell})

\displaystyle=-\,(2\ell)\,(\underline{\omega}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell-1})\qquad

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,(\underline{\omega}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell})

\displaystyle=-\,(m+2\ell-1)\,(\underline{\omega}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell-1})\quad

\displaystyle,\quad

\displaystyle 2\ell-1\geq-m+1

\displaystyle\underline{D}\,(\underline{\omega}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell+1})

\displaystyle=-\,(m+2\ell)\,(\underline{\omega}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell})\qquad

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,(\underline{\omega}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell+1})

\displaystyle=-\,(2\ell+1)\,(\underline{\omega}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell})\quad

\displaystyle,\quad

\displaystyle 2\ell\geq-m+1

10.2
Integer powers of
\underline{x}
as finite part (signum)distributions

For
k<-m+1
the functions
\underline{x}^{k}
are no longer locally integrable in
\mathbb{R}^{m}
and thus no longer regular (signum)distributions. Whence the need for a definition.

Definition 13
.

One defines the distributions

\displaystyle{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\ell}\,T_{2\ell}\quad,\quad 2\ell<-m+1

\displaystyle{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\ell}\,U_{2\ell+1}\quad,\quad 2\ell<-m

and the signumdistributions

\displaystyle{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\ell}\,{}^{s}T_{2\ell}\quad,\quad 2\ell<-m+1

\displaystyle{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell+1}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\ell}\,{}^{s}U_{2\ell+1}\quad,\quad 2\ell<-m

Note that

\left({\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell}\right)^{\vee}=(-1)^{\ell}\,{}^{s}U_{2\ell}\qquad\left({\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell+1}\right)^{\vee}=(-1)^{\ell+1}\,{}^{s}T_{2\ell+1}

and

\left({\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell}\right)^{\wedge}=(-1)^{\ell+1}\,U_{2\ell}\qquad\left({\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell+1}\right)^{\wedge}=(-1)^{\ell}\,T_{2\ell+1}

and observe that none of those four associated (signum)distributions are integer powers of
\underline{x}
.

Remark 9
.

We will keep the notations
{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{k}
and
{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{k}
even when
\underline{x}^{k}
is locally integrable.

As to division by the vector variable
\underline{x}
the following formulæ may be easily calculated:

•

\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell}={\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell-1}

•

\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell+1}={\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell}\quad,\quad 2\ell\neq-m

•

\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m+1}=[{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m}]={\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m}+\delta(\underline{x})\,c\quad,\quad m\ {\rm even}

10.3
Complex powers of
\underline{x}
as distributions

Through the
T_{\lambda}
and
U_{\lambda}
distributions it is possible to define
complex
powers of the vector variable
\underline{x}
as distributions.

Definition 14
.

For
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
one puts

(i\,\underline{x})^{\lambda}=\frac{1}{2}\,(1+\exp{(i\pi\lambda)})\,T_{\lambda}+\frac{1}{2}\,i\,(1-\exp{(i\pi\lambda)})\,U_{\lambda}

Remark 10
.

It has to be checked that in the particular case where
\lambda
is an integer the distributions introduced above are recovered. We indeed have:

•

for
\lambda=2\ell,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
we get

(i\,\underline{x})^{2\ell}=T_{2\ell}=(-1)^{\ell}\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell}

•

for
\lambda=2\ell+1,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
we get

(i\,\underline{x})^{2\ell+1}=i\,U_{2\ell+1}=i\,(-1)^{\ell}\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{2\ell+1}

Now we investigate the holomorphy of
(i\,\underline{x})^{\lambda}
in the complex
\lambda
–plane. Recall that

•

T_{\lambda}
is a meromorphic function of
\lambda
showing simple poles at
\lambda=-m-2\ell,\ell=0,1,2,\ldots

•

U_{\lambda}
is a meromorphic function of
\lambda
showing simple poles at
\lambda=-m-2\ell-1,\ell=0,1,2,\ldots

So it becomes clear that possible singularities of the distributions
(i\,\underline{x})^{\lambda}
depend on the parity of the dimension
m
.

If the dimension
m
is
odd
, then

(i\,\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell}=i\,U_{-m-2\ell}

and

(i\,\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell-1}=T_{-m-2\ell-1}

implying that the distribution
(i\,\underline{x})^{\lambda}
then is an
entire
function of
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
showing no singularities at all.

If the dimension
m
is
even
, then

(i\,\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell}=T_{-m-2\ell}

and

(i\,\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell-1}=i\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}

implying that the distribution
(i\,\underline{x})^{\lambda}
then is a meromorphic function of
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
showing simple poles at
\lambda=-m,-m-1,-m-2,\ldots
. But note that at those singular points the distribution
(i\,\underline{x})^{\lambda}
still is defined through the monomial pseudofunctions, however
not
turning this distribution into an entire function of
\lambda
.

Now we compute the Dirac derivative of the distribution
(i\,\underline{x})^{\lambda}
. We obtain in general

\displaystyle\underline{\partial}\,(i\,\underline{x})^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\,(1-\exp{(i\pi(\lambda-1))})\,\underline{\partial}\,T_{\lambda}+\frac{1}{2}\,i\,(1+\exp{(i\pi(\lambda-1))})\,\underline{\partial}\,U_{\lambda}{}

\displaystyle=(-i)\,\lambda\,(i\,\underline{x})^{\lambda-1}-\frac{1}{2}\,i\,(m-1)(1-\exp{(i\pi\,\lambda)})\,T_{\lambda-1}

(107)

This formula (
107
) is valid in the whole complex
\lambda
–plane when the dimension
m
is odd, and valid in
\mathbb{C}\backslash\{-m+1,-m,-m-1,\ldots\}
when
m
is even.
For the singular values of the parameter
\lambda
we obtain the following results.

•

\boxed{\lambda=-m+1}

We consider the distribution

(i\,\underline{x})^{-m+1}=\frac{1}{2}\,(1-(-1)^{m})\,T_{-m+1}+\frac{1}{2}\,i\,(1+(-1)^{m})\,U_{-m+1}

For odd dimension
m
we do not expect singularities to appear and indeed we get

(i\,\underline{x})^{-m+1}=T_{-m+1}

with

\underline{\partial}\,(i\,\underline{x})^{-m+1}=(-m+1)\,U_{-m}

whence

\underline{\partial}\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m+1}=(m-1)\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m}

For even dimension
m
we get

(i\,\underline{x})^{-m+1}=i\,U_{-m+1}

with

\underline{\partial}\,(i\,\underline{x})^{-m+1}=i\,(-a_{m})\,\delta(\underline{x})

whence

\underline{\partial}\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m+1}=(-1)^{m/2+1}\,a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})

•

\boxed{\lambda=-m}

We consider the distribution

(i\,\underline{x})^{-m}=\frac{1}{2}\,(1+(-1)^{m})\,T_{-m}+\frac{1}{2}\,i\,(1-(-1)^{m})\,U_{-m}

For odd dimension
m
we do not expect singularities to appear and indeed we get

(i\,\underline{x})^{-m}=i\,U_{-m}

with

\underline{\partial}\,(i\,\underline{x})^{-m}=i\,T_{-m-1}

whence

\underline{\partial}\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m}={\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m-1}

For even dimension
m
we get

(i\,\underline{x})^{-m}=T_{-m}

with

\underline{\partial}\,(i\,\underline{x})^{-m}=(-m)\,U_{-m-1}-\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}\,\delta(\underline{x})

whence

\underline{\partial}\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m}=m\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m-1}+(-1)^{m/2+1}\,\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}\,\delta(\underline{x})

•

\boxed{\lambda=-m-2\ell}

We consider the distribution

(i\,\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell}=\frac{1}{2}\,(1+(-1)^{m})\,T_{-m-2\ell}+\frac{1}{2}\,i\,(1-(-1)^{m})\,U_{-m-2\ell}

For odd dimension
m
we do not expect singularities to appear and indeed we get

(i\,\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell}=i\,U_{-m-2\ell}

with

\underline{\partial}\,(i\,\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell}=i\,(2\ell+1)\,T_{-m-2\ell-1}

whence

\underline{\partial}\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell}=(2\ell+1)\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell-1}

For even dimension
m
we get

(i\,\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell}=T_{-m-2\ell}

with

\underline{\partial}\,(i\,\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell}=(-m-2\ell)\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{1}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

whence

\underline{\partial}\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell}=(m+2\ell)\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{m/2+1}\,\frac{1}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\,\delta(\underline{x})

•

\boxed{\lambda=-m-2\ell-1}

We consider the distribution

(i\,\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell-1}=\frac{1}{2}\,(1+(-1)^{m+1})\,T_{-m-2\ell-1}+\frac{1}{2}\,i\,(1-(-1)^{m+1})\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}

For odd dimension
m
we do not expect singularities to appear and indeed we get

(i\,\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell-1}=T_{-m-2\ell-1}

with

\underline{\partial}\,(i\,\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell-1}=-\,(m+2\ell+1)\,U_{-m-2\ell-2}

whence

\underline{\partial}\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell-1}=(m+2\ell+1)\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell-2}

For even dimension
m
we get

(i\,\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell-1}=i\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}

with

\underline{\partial}\,(i\,\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell-1}=i\,(2\ell+2)\,T_{-m-2\ell-2}+i\,(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{m+2\ell+2}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

whence

\underline{\partial}\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell-1}=(2\ell+2)\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell-2}+(-1)^{m/2+1}\,\frac{m+2\ell+2}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+2}\,\delta(\underline{x})

We may also compute the action of the operator
\underline{D}
on the complex powers of the vector variable
\underline{x}
. Recall that the operator
\underline{D}
is the cartesian signum–partner operator to the Dirac operator
\underline{\partial}
. We obtain in general

\displaystyle\underline{D}\,(i\,\underline{x})^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\,(1+\exp{(i\pi\lambda)})\,\underline{D}\,T_{\lambda}+\frac{1}{2}\,i\,(1-\exp{(i\pi\lambda)})\,\underline{D}\,U_{\lambda}{}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\,(1+\exp{(i\pi\lambda)})\,(\lambda+m-1)\,U_{\lambda-1}-\frac{1}{2}\,i\,(1-\exp{(i\pi\lambda)})\,\lambda\,T_{\lambda-1}

(108)

This formula (
108
) is valid in the whole complex
\lambda
–plane when the dimension
m
is odd, and valid in
\mathbb{C}\backslash\{-m+1,-m,-m-1,\ldots\}
when
m
is even. For the singular values of the parameter
\lambda
we obtain the following expressions.

•

[formula]

For odd dimension
m
we do not expect singularities to appear and indeed we find

\displaystyle\underline{D}\,(i\underline{x})^{-m+1}=\underline{D}\,T_{-m+1}=0

whence

\underline{D}\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m+1}=0

For even dimension
m
we find

\underline{D}\,(i\underline{x})^{-m+1}=i\,\underline{D}\,U_{-m+1}=i\,(m-1)\,T_{-m}-i\,a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})

whence

\underline{D}\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m+1}=(m-1)\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m}+(-1)^{-m/2+1}\,a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})

•

[formula]

For odd dimension
m
we do not expect singularities to appear and indeed we find

\underline{D}\,(i\underline{x})^{-m}=i\,\underline{D}\,U_{-m}=i\,m\,T_{-m-1}

whence

\underline{D}\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m}=m\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m-1}

For even dimension
m
we find

\underline{D}\,(i\underline{x})^{-m}=\underline{D}\,T_{-m}=-\,U_{-m-1}-\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})

whence

\underline{D}\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m}={\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m-1}+(-1)^{-m/2+1}\,\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})

•

[formula]

For odd dimension
m
we do not expect singularities to appear and indeed we find

\underline{D}\,(i\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell}=i\,\underline{D}\,U_{-m-2\ell}=i\,(m+2\ell)\,T_{-m-2\ell-1}

whence

\underline{D}\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell}=(m+2\ell)\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell-1}

For even dimension
m
we find

\underline{D}\,(i\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell}=\underline{D}\,T_{-m-2\ell}=-\,(2\ell+1)\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}+\frac{(-1)^{\ell+1}}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})

whence

\underline{D}\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell}=(2\ell+1)\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{-m/2+1}\,\frac{1}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})

•

[formula]

For odd dimension
m
we do not expect singularities to appear and indeed we find

\underline{D}\,(i\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell-1}=\underline{D}\,T_{-m-2\ell-1}=-\,(2\ell+2)\,U_{-m-2\ell-2}

whence

\underline{D}\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell-1}=(2\ell+2)\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell-2}

For even dimension
m
we find

\underline{D}\,(i\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell-1}=i\,\underline{D}\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}=i\,(m+2\ell+1)\,T_{-m-2\ell-2}+i\,(-1)^{\ell}\,\frac{m+2\ell+2}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+2}\delta(\underline{x})

whence

\underline{D}\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell-1}=(m+2\ell+1)\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell-2}+(-1)^{-m/2-1}\,\frac{m+2\ell+2}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+2}\delta(\underline{x})

10.4
Complex powers of
\underline{x}
as signumdistributions

Similarly as in the preceding subsection we can define signumdistributions involving complex powers of
\underline{x}
.

Definition 15
.

For
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
one puts

{}^{s}(i\,\underline{x})^{\lambda}=\frac{1}{2}\,(1+\exp{(i\pi\lambda)})\,^{s}T_{\lambda}+\frac{1}{2}\,i\,(1-\exp{(i\pi\lambda)})\,^{s}U_{\lambda}

Remark 11
.

It has to be checked that for integer values of the complex parameter
\lambda
we recover signumdistributions already introduced above. We indeed have

•

for
\lambda=2\ell,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}

{}^{s}(i\,\underline{x})^{2\ell}=\,^{s}T_{2\ell}=(-1)^{\ell}\,{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell}

•

for
\lambda=2\ell+1,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}

{}^{s}(i\,\underline{x})^{2\ell+1}=i\,^{s}U_{2\ell+1}=i\,(-1)^{\ell}\,{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{2\ell+1}

Now we investigate the holomorphy of
{}^{s}(i\,\underline{x})^{\lambda}
in the complex
\lambda
–plane. Recall that

•

{}^{s}T_{\lambda}
is a meromorphic function of
\lambda
showing simple poles at
\lambda=-m-2\ell-1,\ell=0,1,2,\ldots

•

{}^{s}U_{\lambda}
is a meromorphic function of
\lambda
showing simple poles at
\lambda=-m-2\ell,\ell=0,1,2,\ldots

So it becomes clear that possible singularities of the signumdistributions
{}^{s}(i\,\underline{x})^{\lambda}
depend on the parity of the dimension
m
.

If the dimension
m
is
even
, then

{}^{s}(i\,\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell}=\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell}

and

{}^{s}(i\,\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell-1}=\,i\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell-1}

implying that the signumdistribution
{}^{s}(i\,\underline{x})^{\lambda}
then is an
entire
function of
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
showing no singularities at all.

If the dimension
m
is
odd
, then

{}^{s}(i\,\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell}=i\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}

and

{}^{s}(i\,\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell-1}=\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-1}

implying that the signumdistribution
{}^{s}(i\,\underline{x})^{\lambda}
then is a meromorphic function of
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
showing simple poles at
\lambda=-m,-m-1,-m-2,\ldots
. But note that at those singular points the signumdistribution
{}^{s}(i\,\underline{x})^{\lambda}
still is defined through the monomial pseudofunctions, however
not
turning this signumdistribution into an entire function of
\lambda
.

Now we compute the Dirac derivative of the signumdistribution
{}^{s}(i\,\underline{x})^{\lambda}
. In general we obtain

\underline{\partial}\,^{s}(i\underline{x})^{\lambda}=\frac{1}{2}\,(1+\exp{(i\pi\lambda)})\,\lambda\,^{s}U_{\lambda-1}-\frac{1}{2}\,i\,(1-\exp{(i\pi\lambda)})\,(\lambda+m-1)\,^{s}T_{\lambda-1}

(109)

This formula (
109
) is valid in the whole complex
\lambda
–plane when the dimension
m
is even, while for odd dimension
m
it is valid for
\lambda\neq-m+1,-m,-m-1,-m-2,\ldots
. For those singular values of the parameter
\lambda
we obtain the following results.

•

[formula]

For even dimension
m
we do not expect singularities to appear and indeed we find

\underline{\partial}\,^{s}(i\underline{x})^{-m+1}=i\,\underline{\partial}\,^{s}U_{-m+1}=0

whence

\underline{\partial}\,{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{-m+1}=0

For odd dimension
m
we find

\underline{\partial}\,^{s}(i\underline{x})^{-m+1}=\underline{\partial}\,^{s}T_{-m+1}=-\,(m-1)\,^{s}U_{-m}+a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})

whence

\underline{\partial}\,{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{-m+1}=(m-1)\,{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{-m}+(-1)^{\frac{-m+1}{2}}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})

•

[formula]

For even dimension
m
we do not expect singularities to appear and indeed we find

\underline{\partial}\,^{s}(i\underline{x})^{-m}=\underline{\partial}\,^{s}T_{-m}=-\,m\,^{s}U_{-m-1}

whence

\underline{\partial}\,{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{-m}=m\,{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{-m-1}

For odd dimension
m
we find

\underline{\partial}\,^{s}(i\underline{x})^{-m}=i\,\underline{\partial}\,^{s}U_{-m}=i\,^{s}T_{-m-1}-i\,\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})

whence

\underline{\partial}\,{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{-m}={\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{-m-1}+(-1)^{\frac{-m+1}{2}}\,\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})

•

[formula]

For even dimension
m
we do not expect singularities to appear and indeed we find

\underline{\partial}\,^{s}(i\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell}=\underline{\partial}\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell}=(-m-2\ell)\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell-1}

whence

\underline{\partial}\,{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell}=(m+2\ell)\,{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell-1}

For odd dimension
m
we find

\underline{\partial}\,^{s}(i\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell}=i\,\underline{\partial}\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}=i\,\left((2\ell+1)\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{1}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})\right)

whence

\underline{\partial}\,{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell}=(2\ell+1)\,{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\frac{-m+1}{2}}\,\frac{1}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})

•

[formula]

For even dimension
m
we do not expect singularities to appear and indeed we find

\underline{\partial}\,^{s}(i\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell-1}=i\,\underline{\partial}\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell-1}=i\,(2\ell+2)\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-2}

whence

\underline{\partial}\,{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell-1}=(2\ell+2)\,{\rm Fp}\,\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell-2}

For odd dimension
m
we find

\underline{\partial}\,^{s}(i\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell-1}=\underline{\partial}\,^{s}T_{-m2\ell-1}=-\,(m+2\ell+1)\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell-2}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{m+2\ell+2}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+2}\delta(\underline{x})

whence

\underline{\partial}\,{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell-1}=(m+2\ell+1)\,{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell-2}+(-1)^{\frac{-m+1}{2}}\,\frac{m+2\ell+2}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+2}\delta(\underline{x})

Finally we can compute the action of the operator
\underline{D}
on the signumdistribution
{}^{s}(i\,\underline{x})^{\lambda}
. In general we obtain

\underline{D}\,^{s}(i\underline{x})^{\lambda}=\frac{1}{2}\,(1+\exp{(i\pi\lambda)})\,(\lambda+m-1)\,^{s}U_{\lambda-1}-\frac{1}{2}\,i\,(1-\exp{(i\pi\lambda)})\,\lambda\,^{s}T_{\lambda-1}

(110)

This formula (
110
) is valid in the whole complex
\lambda
–plane when the dimension
m
is even, while for odd dimension
m
it is valid for
\lambda\neq-m+1,-m,-m-1,-m-2,\ldots
. For those singular values of the parameter
\lambda
we obtain the following results.

•

[formula]

For even dimension
m
we do not expect singularities to appear and indeed we find

\underline{D}\,^{s}(i\underline{x})^{-m+1}=i\,\underline{D}\,^{s}U_{-m+1}=i\,(m-1)\,^{s}T_{-m}

whence

\underline{D}\,{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{-m+1}=(m-1)\,{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{-m}

For odd dimension
m
we find

\underline{D}\,^{s}(i\underline{x})^{-m+1}=\underline{D}\,^{s}T_{-m+1}=a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})

whence

\underline{D}\,{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{-m+1}=(-1)^{\frac{-m+1}{2}}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\delta(\underline{x})

•

[formula]

For even dimension
m
we do not expect singularities to appear and indeed we find

\underline{D}\,^{s}(i\underline{x})^{-m}=\underline{D}\,^{s}T_{-m}=-\,^{s}U_{-m-1}

whence

\underline{D}\,{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{-m}={\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{-m-1}

For odd dimension
m
we find

\underline{D}\,^{s}(i\underline{x})^{-m}=i\,\underline{D}\,^{s}U_{-m}=i\,\left(m\,^{s}T_{-m-1}-\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})\right)

whence

\underline{D}\,{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{-m}=m\,{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{-m-1}+(-1)^{\frac{-m+1}{2}}\,\frac{1}{m}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}\delta(\underline{x})

•

[formula]

For even dimension
m
we do not expect singularities to appear and indeed we find

\underline{D}\,^{s}(i\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell}=\underline{D}\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell}=-\,(2\ell+1)\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell-1}

whence

\underline{D}\,{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell}=(2\ell+1)\,{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell-1}

For odd dimension
m
we find

\underline{D}\,^{s}(i\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell}=i\,\underline{D}\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}=i\,\left((m+2\ell)\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{1}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})\right)

whence

\underline{D}\,{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell}=(m+2\ell)\,{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell-1}+(-1)^{\frac{-m+1}{2}}\,\frac{1}{C(m,\ell)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})

•

[formula]

For even dimension
m
we do not expect singularities to appear and indeed we find

\underline{D}\,^{s}(i\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell-1}=i\,\underline{D}\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell-1}=i\,(m+2\ell+1)\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-2}

whence

\underline{D}\,{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell-1}=(m+2\ell+1)\,{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell-2}

For odd dimension
m
we find

\underline{D}\,^{s}(i\underline{x})^{-m-2\ell-1}=\underline{D}\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-1}=-\,(2\ell+2)\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell-2}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\,\frac{m+2\ell+2}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+2}\delta(\underline{x})

whence

\underline{D}\,{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell-1}=(2\ell+2)\,{\rm Fp}\,^{s}\underline{x}^{-m-2\ell-2}+(-1)^{\frac{-m+1}{2}}\,\frac{m+2\ell+2}{C(m,\ell+1)}\,a_{m}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+2}\delta(\underline{x})

11
Normalization of the distributions
T_{\lambda}
and
U_{\lambda}

As we know the distributions
T_{\lambda}
, considered as functions of the complex parameter
\lambda
, show simple poles at
\lambda=-m,-m-2,-m-4,\ldots
, while the distributions
U_{\lambda}
show simple poles at
\lambda=-m-1,-m-3,-m-5,\ldots
. In these singular points the distributions
T_{\lambda}
and
U_{\lambda}
were defined through the monomial pseudofunctions.

There is however a second option to cope with these singularities: removing the singularities by dividing the distributions
T_{\lambda}
and
U_{\lambda}
by an appropriate Gamma-function. This gives rise to the so–called
normalized distributions

T^{*}_{\lambda}
and
U^{*}_{\lambda}
. Their definition runs as follows.

The normalized distributions
T^{*}_{\lambda}
are defined by

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle{T_{\lambda}^{*}=\pi^{\frac{\lambda+m}{2}}\frac{T_{\lambda}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\lambda+m}{2}\right)}},&\lambda\neq-m-2\ell\\[14.22636pt]
\displaystyle{T_{-m-2\ell}^{*}=\frac{\pi^{\frac{m}{2}-\ell}}{2^{2l}\Gamma\left(\frac{m}{2}+\ell\right)}\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\delta(\underline{x})},&\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\end{array}\right.

The normalized distributions
U^{*}_{\lambda}
are defined by

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle{U_{\lambda}^{*}=\pi^{\frac{\lambda+m+1}{2}}\,\frac{U_{\lambda}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\lambda+m+1}{2}\right)}},&\lambda\neq-m-2\ell-1\\[14.22636pt]
\displaystyle{U_{-m-2\ell-1}^{*}=-\frac{\pi^{\frac{m}{2}-\ell}}{2^{2\ell+1}\,\Gamma\left(\frac{m}{2}+\ell+1\right)}\;\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})},&\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\end{array}\right.

The normalized distributions
T_{\lambda}^{*}
and
U_{\lambda}^{*}
turn out to be holomorphic mappings from
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
to the space
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
of tempered distributions. They are intertwined by the actions of the multiplication operator
\underline{x}
and of the Dirac operator, according to the following formulæ: for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
one has

(i)

\underline{x}\;T_{\lambda}^{*}=\frac{\lambda+m}{2\pi}\;U_{\lambda+1}^{*}
;
\underline{x}\;U_{\lambda}^{*}=U_{\lambda}^{*}\;\underline{x}=-T_{\lambda+1}^{*}

(ii)

\underline{\partial}\;T_{\lambda}^{*}=\lambda\;U_{\lambda-1}^{*}
;
\underline{\partial}\;U_{\lambda}^{*}=U_{\lambda}^{*}\;\underline{\partial}=-2\pi\;T_{\lambda-1}^{*}

(iii)

\Delta T_{\lambda}^{*}=2\pi\lambda T_{\lambda-2}^{*}
;
\Delta U_{\lambda}^{*}=2\pi(\lambda-1)U_{\lambda-2}^{*}

(iv)

{\mathcal{F}}\left[T_{\lambda}^{*}\right]=T_{-\lambda-m}^{*}
;
{\mathcal{F}}\left[U_{\lambda}^{*}\right]=-i\;U_{-\lambda-m}^{*}
.

where, in property (iv), the following definition of the Fourier transformation has been adopted:

{\mathcal{F}}[f(\underline{x})](\underline{y})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}f(\underline{x})\exp\left(-2\pi i\,\langle\underline{x},\underline{y}\rangle\right)\,d\underline{x}.

For an in-depth study of the normalized distributions
T_{\lambda}^{*}
and
U_{\lambda}^{*}
we refer to e.g.
[
11
]
. Additionally we can investigate the behaviour of the
T_{\lambda}^{*}
and
U_{\lambda}^{*}
distributions under the action of the operators
\frac{1}{\underline{x}}
and
\underline{D}
.

Proposition 4
.

One has

(i)
\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,T^{*}_{\lambda}=-\,U^{*}_{\lambda-1}
, for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}

(ii)
\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,U^{*}_{\lambda}=\frac{2\pi}{\lambda+m-1}\,T^{*}_{\lambda-1}
, for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
except for
\lambda=-m+1

Proof

(i) For
\lambda\neq-m-2\ell
we have

\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,T^{*}_{\lambda}=\pi^{\frac{\lambda+m}{2}}\frac{-\,U_{\lambda-1}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\lambda+m}{2}\right)}=-\,U^{*}_{\lambda-1}

(i’) For
\lambda=-m-2\ell
we have

\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,T^{*}_{-m-2\ell}=\frac{\pi^{\frac{m}{2}-\ell}}{2^{2l}\Gamma\left(\frac{m}{2}+\ell\right)}\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\delta(\underline{x})=\frac{\pi^{\frac{m}{2}-\ell}}{2^{2l}\Gamma\left(\frac{m}{2}+\ell\right)}\,\frac{1}{m+2\ell}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})=-\,U^{*}_{-m-2\ell-1}

(ii) For
\lambda\neq-m-2\ell+1
we have

\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,U^{*}_{\lambda}=\pi^{\frac{\lambda+m+1}{2}}\,\frac{T_{\lambda-1}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\lambda+m+1}{2}\right)}=\frac{2\pi}{\lambda+m-1}\,T^{*}_{\lambda-1}

(ii’) For
\lambda=-m-2\ell-1
we have

\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,U_{-m-2\ell-1}=-\frac{\pi^{\frac{m}{2}-\ell}}{2^{2\ell+1}\,\Gamma\left(\frac{m}{2}+\ell+1\right)}\,\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})=-\frac{\pi^{\frac{m}{2}-\ell}}{2^{2\ell+1}\,\Gamma\left(\frac{m}{2}+\ell+1\right)}\,\frac{1}{2\ell+2}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+2}\delta(\underline{x})=\frac{2\pi}{-2\ell-2}\,T^{*}_{-m-2\ell-2}

\square

Remark 12
.

Up to a constant the distribution
U^{*}_{-m+1}
equals the distribution
U_{-m+1}
, which, recall, is a locally integrable function. We know that
\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,U_{-m+1}
equals an equivalence class of distributions, viz.

\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,U_{-m+1}=T_{-m}+\delta(\underline{x})\,c=T_{-m}+T^{*}_{-m}\,c^{\prime}

which clearly, due to the appearance of
T_{-m}
, do not belong tot the
T^{*}_{\lambda}
family of distributions. This is the reason why we have left out the case
\lambda=-m+1
in the above proposition.

Proposition 5
.

One has

(i)
\underline{D}\,T^{*}_{\lambda}=(\lambda+m-1)\,U^{*}_{\lambda-1}
, for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}

(ii)
\underline{D}\,U^{*}_{\lambda}=-\,\frac{2\pi\lambda}{\lambda+m-1}\,T^{*}_{\lambda-1}
, for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
except for
\lambda=-m+1

Proof

(i) As
T^{*}_{\lambda}
is a radial distribution we have for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
that

\underline{D}\,T^{*}_{\lambda}=\left(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r}-(m-1)\,\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\right)\,T^{*}_{\lambda}=\underline{\partial}\,T^{*}_{\lambda}-(m-1)\,\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,T^{*}_{\lambda}=(\lambda+m-1)\,U^{*}_{\lambda-1}

(ii) For
\lambda\neq-m-2\ell-1
we have

\underline{D}\,U^{*}_{\lambda}=\left(\underline{\omega}\,\partial_{r}-\frac{1}{r}\,\partial_{\underline{\omega}}-(m-1)\,\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\right)\,U^{*}_{\lambda}=\frac{\pi^{\frac{\lambda+m+1}{2}}}{\Gamma{(\frac{\lambda+m+1}{2})}}\,(-\lambda+m-1)\,T_{\lambda-1}-(m-1)\,\frac{2\pi}{\lambda+m-1}\,T^{*}_{\lambda-1}=-\,\frac{2\pi\lambda}{\lambda+m-1}\,T^{*}_{\lambda-1}

(ii’) For
\lambda=-m-2\ell-1
we have

\underline{D}\,U^{*}_{-m-2\ell-1}=-\frac{\pi^{\frac{m}{2}-\ell}}{2^{2\ell+1}\,\Gamma\left(\frac{m}{2}+\ell+1\right)}\;\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\left(\frac{m+2\ell+1}{2\ell+2}+\frac{m-1}{2\ell+2}-\frac{m-1}{2\ell+2}\right)\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+2}\delta(\underline{x})=-\,2\pi\,\frac{m+2\ell+1}{2\ell+2}\,T^{*}_{-m-2\ell-2}

\square

In a similar way the signumdistributions
{}^{s}T_{\lambda}
and
{}^{s}U_{\lambda}
may now be normalized. Recall that
{}^{s}T_{\lambda}
shows simple poles at
\lambda=-m-1,-m-3,\ldots
, while
{}^{s}U_{\lambda}
shows simple poles at
\lambda=-m,-m-2,\ldots
. We define:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle{{}^{s}T_{\lambda}^{*}=\pi^{\frac{\lambda+m+1}{2}}\,\frac{{}^{s}T_{\lambda}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\lambda+m+1}{2}\right)}},&\lambda\neq-m-2\ell-1\\[14.22636pt]
\displaystyle{{}^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-1}^{*}=\frac{\pi^{\frac{m}{2}-\ell}}{2^{2\ell+1}\,\Gamma\left(\frac{m}{2}+\ell+1\right)}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})},&\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\end{array}\right.

and

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle{{}^{s}U_{\lambda}^{*}=\pi^{\frac{\lambda+m}{2}}\frac{{}^{s}U_{\lambda}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\lambda+m}{2}\right)}},&\lambda\neq-m-2\ell\\[14.22636pt]
\displaystyle{{}^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}^{*}=\frac{\pi^{\frac{m}{2}-\ell}}{2^{2l}\Gamma\left(\frac{m}{2}+\ell\right)}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\delta(\underline{x})},&\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\end{array}\right.

Lemma 2
.

For all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
one has that

(T_{\lambda}^{*})^{\vee}=\underline{\omega}\,T_{\lambda}^{*}=\,^{s}U_{\lambda}^{*}

and

(U_{\lambda}^{*})^{\vee}=\underline{\omega}\,U_{\lambda}^{*}=-\,^{s}T_{\lambda}^{*}

Proposition 6
.

For all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
one has that

\underline{x}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}^{*}=\,^{s}U^{*}_{\lambda+1}

and

\underline{x}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}^{*}=-\,\frac{\lambda+m}{2\pi}\,^{s}T^{*}_{\lambda+1}

Proof

Obviously these result follow by simple transition to the associated signumdistributions of the corresponding formulæ for the distributions
T^{*}_{\lambda}
and
U^{*}_{\lambda}
, the multiplication operator
\underline{x}
being a signum–self–adjoint operator. However let us give a direct proof.

(i) For
\lambda\neq-m-2\ell-1
one has
\underline{x}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}^{*}=\pi^{\frac{\lambda+m+1}{2}}\,\frac{\underline{x}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\lambda+m+1}{2}\right)}=\pi^{\frac{\lambda+m+1}{2}}\,\frac{{}^{s}U_{\lambda}+1}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\lambda+m+1}{2}\right)}=\,^{s}U^{*}_{\lambda+1}
.

(ii)
\underline{x}\,^{s}T_{-m-2\ell-1}^{*}=\frac{\pi^{\frac{m}{2}-\ell}}{2^{2\ell+1}\,\Gamma\left(\frac{m}{2}+\ell+1\right)}\,\underline{x}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell+1}\delta(\underline{x})=\frac{\pi^{\frac{m}{2}-\ell}}{2^{2\ell+1}\,\Gamma\left(\frac{m}{2}+\ell+1\right)}\,\underline{\omega}\,(m+2\ell)\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\delta(\underline{x})=\frac{\pi^{\frac{m}{2}-\ell}}{2^{2\ell}\,\Gamma\left(\frac{m}{2}+\ell\right)}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\delta(\underline{x})=\,^{s}U^{*}_{-m-2\ell}

(iii) For
\lambda\neq-m-2\ell
one has
\underline{x}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}^{*}=\pi^{\frac{\lambda+m}{2}}\frac{\underline{x}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\lambda+m}{2}\right)}=-\,\pi^{\frac{\lambda+m}{2}}\frac{{}^{s}T_{\lambda}+1}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\lambda+m}{2}\right)}=-\,\frac{\lambda+m}{2\pi}\,^{s}T^{*}_{\lambda+1}
.

(iv)
\underline{x}\,^{s}U_{-m-2\ell}^{*}=\frac{\pi^{\frac{m}{2}-\ell}}{2^{2l}\Gamma\left(\frac{m}{2}+\ell\right)}\,\underline{x}\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell}\delta(\underline{x})=\frac{\pi^{\frac{m}{2}-\ell}}{2^{2l}\Gamma\left(\frac{m}{2}+\ell\right)}\,(2\ell)\,\underline{\omega}\,\underline{\partial}^{2\ell-1}\delta(\underline{x})=\frac{\ell}{\pi}\,^{s}T^{*}_{-m-2\ell+1}
.

\square

Proposition 7
.

For all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
one has that

\underline{D}\,^{s}T_{\lambda}^{*}=2\pi\,^{s}U_{\lambda-1}^{*}

and

\underline{D}\,^{s}U_{\lambda}^{*}=-\,\lambda\,^{s}T^{*}_{\lambda-1}

Proof

These results simply follow from the corresponding formulæ for the distributions
T^{*}_{\lambda}
and
U^{*}_{\lambda}
, the operator
\underline{D}
being the signum–adjoint operator of the Dirac operator
\underline{\partial}
.

\square

In the similar way the results of the propositions
4
and
5
lead to the following formulæ.

Proposition 8
.

(i) For
\lambda\neq-m+1
one has

\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,^{s}T^{*}_{\lambda}=-\,\frac{2\pi}{\lambda+m-1}\,^{s}U^{*}_{\lambda-1}
and
\underline{\partial}\,^{s}T^{*}_{\lambda}=\frac{2\pi\lambda}{\lambda+m-1}\,^{s}U^{*}_{\lambda-1}
.

(ii) For all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
one has

\frac{1}{\underline{x}}\,^{s}U^{*}_{\lambda}=^{s}T^{*}_{\lambda-1}
and
\underline{\partial}\,^{s}U^{*}_{\lambda}=-\,(\lambda+m-1)\,^{s}T^{*}_{\lambda-1}
.

12
Physics in threedimensional Euclidean space

Consider standard three dimensional Euclidean space
(m=3)
. We will constantly switch between Clifford algebra and vector algebra notation, identifying the Clifford one–vector
\underline{v}
with the algebraic vector
\vec{v}
. Note that for the scalar product of two algebraic vectors it holds that
\vec{v}\circ\vec{w}=-\,\underline{v}\cdot\underline{w}
. In particular we identify the Dirac operator
\underline{\partial}
with the gradient operator
\vec{\nabla}
. Alternative notations used are
\vec{e}_{r}
for
\underline{\omega}
,
r^{\lambda}
for
T_{\lambda}
and
\vec{e}_{r}\,r^{\lambda}
for
U_{\lambda}
. In this section we will discuss some well–known formulae from physics.

12.1

A vector field
\vec{F}
in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
is called
rotation free
(or
irrotational
) in an open region
\Omega
if it is continuously differentiable in
\Omega
and satisfies

\vec{\nabla}\times\vec{F}=0

Invoking Stokes’s Theorem it holds that the line integral of a rotation free vector field over a closed smooth path
\mathcal{C}
in
\Omega
vanishes:

\oint_{\mathcal{C}}\,\vec{F}\,\circ\vec{dP}=0

If
\vec{F}
stands for a force field then the above line integral represents the
work
done by this field to move an object around the path
\mathcal{C}
. This explains why e.g. a planet’s orbiting around the sun in the Newtonian gravitational field, which is proportional to

\vec{F}_{grav}=\frac{\vec{e}_{r}}{r^{2}}=\frac{\vec{x}}{|\vec{x}|^{3}}

and is rotation free in
\mathbb{R}^{3}\backslash\{O\}
, does not cost any energy.

In Clifford algebra notation, this gravitational field
\vec{F}_{grav}
takes the form, with dimension
m=3
,

\underline{F}_{grav}=\frac{\underline{\omega}}{r^{2}}=U_{-m+1}

The irrotationality of
\vec{F}_{grav}
in
\mathbb{R}^{3}\backslash\{O\}
corresponds with

\underline{\partial}\wedge U_{-m+1}=0

which follows from

\underline{\partial}\,U_{-m+1}=-\,a_{m}\,\delta(\underline{x})

the result being a scalar expression. As an aside note that in the complement of the origin it holds that

\underline{\partial}\cdot U_{-m+1}=0

which means that the gravitational field
\vec{F}_{grav}
is also
divergence free
in
\mathbb{R}^{3}\backslash\{O\}
.

But, upon inspection of the formulae obtained in Section
8
, it becomes clear that, for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
,
\underline{\partial}\,U_{\lambda}
is a scalar expression, in other words:

\underline{\partial}\wedge U_{\lambda}=0\quad,\quad\forall\lambda\in\mathbb{C}

which implies that all vector fields of the form
